FINANCE BENCHMARK: DATA DISCOVERY & WORKFLOW GROUNDING. CONSOLIDATED REFERENCE
Long-horizon, spreadsheet-centric finance agent benchmark. Phase 1 (data discovery) + workflow
grounding from practitioner videos. Generated from the repository by poc/build_reference_txt.py.

Repository branch : https://github.com/kv-248/kv-248/tree/claude/finance-benchmark-data-discovery-q6nfcd/finance-benchmark
This file         : finance-benchmark/FINANCE_BENCHMARK_REFERENCE.txt
Transcripts       : finance-benchmark/VIDEO_TRANSCRIPTS.txt (70 videos, timestamped auto-captions)

Contents

  2. What we are building (scope decisions, definitions, selection principle)
  3. Benchmarks surveyed (with links), gaps, coverage map, design implications
  4. Top 10 data sources (links, what we pulled, natural checks, workflows, caveats)
  5. Other sources, mirrors and the avoid list
  6. Access notes (what the sandbox could and could not reach)
  7. Proof-of-concept pulls and the real-data traps they exposed
  8. Reference workflow video: Otis 3-statement model (verified against transcript)
  9. All 71 practitioner videos (link, inputs, deliverable, timestamped steps, checks, judgment)
 10. Insights from the videos
 11. Workflows we can build (same level as the reference; flagship workflows; concept library)
 12. Caveats
 13. Appendix: every URL cited in the research notes

====================================================================================================
2. WHAT WE ARE BUILDING
====================================================================================================

Scope decisions from the kickoff Q&A
  Environment     : spreadsheet-centric; raw inputs (PDF incl. scans, CSV, XML, HTML) are turned into a workbook env
  Domains/personas: all (accounting ops, FP&A, investment research, credit, public finance, audit/regulatory, household/SMB)
  Long horizon    : a big chunk of a professional's job, composed of several finance concepts (e.g. an end-to-end rebalance)
  Licensing       : research use is fine
  Geography       : any / mixed; cross-jurisdiction is a plus
  Messiness       : all, including scanned PDFs
  Env unit        : both deep (one entity, many periods) and broad (a universe of entities)
  Multi-source    : combining sources is encouraged
  Grading         : open; likely LLM judge, with natural numeric checks as a bonus
  Purpose         : separate frontier models; difficulty comes largely from horizon

Definition of a workflow (from mentor guidance)
  A job an analyst carries out independently: gather source documents (filings, PDFs, statements,
  exports) -> extract and structure the numbers in a workbook -> build the analysis -> check it ties
  -> reach a judgment -> hand over a deliverable. Reference level: the Otis 90-minute 3-statement
  case study (section 8).

Seven operations at that level
  GATHER    find and open the right source documents
  EXTRACT   get numbers out of PDFs or exports into the workbook
  STRUCTURE lay out the workbook: periods, line items, conventions
  BUILD     formulas, schedules, projections, links
  CHECK     tie to reported totals; balance and sanity checks
  JUDGE     assumptions, trade-offs, interpreting results
  DELIVER   a conclusion, recommendation or hand-off artifact

Selection principle for data
  Prefer sources where the source itself reports totals that bottom-up work must reproduce
  ("natural ground truth": audited statements vs. ledgers, servicer reports vs. loan tapes, reported
  NAV vs. holdings, the form's own reconciliation lines). That keeps long, messy, real trajectories
  gradeable. Exceptions found on real data are restatements, mergers, special items and sign
  conventions: exactly the judgment points that separate strong models.



====================================================================================================
3. BENCHMARKS SURVEYED (~60; full detail in docs/01_benchmark_survey.md)
====================================================================================================

SpreadsheetBench 2 (highest priority read): 321 tasks (modeling 100, debugging 100, template 97, charts 24); data from Damodaran, Screener.in, BSE India, Bloomberg; experts build gold workbooks then delete regions / inject errors; avg 11.8 sheets and ~594 cells to modify; bash + xlsx viewer, no network, <=50 turns; exact cell match + VLM rubric for charts; best model ~35% (debugging <=12%); one workbook per task, no time dimension.

    link: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14991

    length: 1 to 5 rounds | eval: Cell, OJ-style (3 test cases per item) | size: 912 instr. / 2,729 cases

## 3. What "long-horizon" means in these papers, and how they keep it verifiable

### 3.1 How horizon is measured

The papers use five different yardsticks.

1. Human time:
   - GDPval: about 6.7 h.
   - BankerToolBench: about 5 h (max 21 h).
   - BlueFin: about 5 h of annotation per task.
   - FrontierFinance: 18.3 h on average (max 35 h).
2. Agent steps and tokens:
   - Finance Agent Benchmark: 3.5–12 turns.
   - FinGAIA: 5–10 steps.
   - Finch: median 16 tool calls.
   - Toolathlon: about 20–27 turns.
   - BlueFin: 24–38 turns.
   - TheAgentCompany: 27 steps.
   - SpreadsheetBench 2: 50-turn cap.
   - APEX-Agents: about 92 steps, 250-step timeout.
   - FrontierFinance: 122–302 messages and up to 28M tokens.
   - APEX–Accounting: cap of 500 steps and 5M tokens.
   - BankerToolBench: up to 539 LLM calls.
3. Size of the output:
   - SpreadsheetBench 2: about 594 modified cells, and over 1,100 for modeling tasks.
   - MBABench: about 33× SpreadsheetBench's cells.
   - BankerToolBench and FrontierFinance: about 100–150 rubric criteria per deliverable.
4. Simulated calendar time with carried-forward state:
   - AccountingBench: 12 months.
   - EnterpriseArena: 132 months.
   - Vending-Bench 2 and E-Commerce Bench: 365 days.
   - CEO-Bench: 500 days.
5. Chained subtasks within one workflow:
   - Finch: 78.5% of workflows are multi-task.
   - OfficeBench: multi-app tasks.
   - FinMaster: disclosure items, then full statements.
   - SpreadsheetBench 2: multi-sheet dependency chains.

Observation: only group 4 has *temporal state*, where outputs from period t become inputs at t+1. Within that group, only AccountingBench does real accounting work on real documents, and it is closed, uses one company and is only partly documented. The others are coarse decision simulators scored on a single economic outcome.

### 3.2 Verification strategies, from most to least deterministic

| Strategy | Used by | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|---|
| Final-state / DB assertions + collateral-damage checks | τ²-bench, AppWorld, Toolathlon, CFAgentBench, TheAgentCompany checkpoints | Cheap, reproducible, path-agnostic | Needs a structured end state; weak for documents |
| Exact cell match vs. gold workbook | SpreadsheetBench 2, SpreadsheetBench v1 | Objective | Brittle (undercounts equivalent layouts); one gold answer |
| Multiple test inputs (OJ-style) / perturbation tests | SpreadsheetBench v1 (3 cases per item); BlueFin (change WACC, check EV) | Tests that the *model logic* is live, not hardcoded | Needs formula-preserving outputs |
| Exact balance sheet + ledger replay | FinBalance (BS_exact vs. BS_recon) | Detects "reported ≠ supported"; accounting identities give free checks | Requires a structured JE format |
| Generator/simulator ground truth (oracle) | FinMaster (FinSim), FinBalance, EnterpriseArena, FinalityBench (hidden event log), ERP-Bench (CP-SAT optimum) | Unlimited, contamination-resistant, exact | Synthetic-realism gap |
| Economic outcome metric | Vending-Bench, EnterpriseArena, CEO-Bench, E-Commerce Bench, FinalityBench, SWE-Lancer ($) | One interpretable number | Sparse; hides *why*; high variance |
| In-trajectory validators / gating | AccountingBench (recon must pass to proceed), FrontierFinance (gating condition) | Dense signal, stops runaway errors | Invites reward hacking (fabricated reconciling items) |
| Tolerance bands | FinSearchComp, BlueFin (±2%) | Handles rounding and live data | Choosing the band is a judgment call |
| Weighted binary rubrics + LLM/agent judge | BankerToolBench (88.2%, κ .76), APEX–Accounting (97.1%), BlueFin (α .826), MBABench (0.92), Finch (82–90%), Finance Agent Benchmark, FinDeepResearch, APEX-Agents | Scales to open-ended deliverables (decks, memos) | Judge drift and self-preference (FrontierFinance: r = 0.63 even with a rubric; over-estimates) |
| Pairwise expert preference | GDPval | Gold-standard quality | Expensive, not reproducible |
| Reliability metrics (pass^k) | τ²-bench, APEX–Accounting (Pass^8 2.6%), BankerToolBench (Pass^3), CFAgentBench (pass^5) | Exposes inconsistency hidden by pass@1 | k× cost |

Practices for keeping long runs verifiable:
- Anchor tasks to a historical date, offline (BankerToolBench). Avoid live data, which hurt MCP-Universe and FinSearchComp reproducibility.
- Score at checkpoints or per period rather than only at the end (TheAgentCompany; AccountingBench per month).
- Decompose deliverables into atomic binary criteria.
- Make judges *agentic* so they can open and run the files (BankerToolBench's Gandalf, BlueFin).
- Run perturbation or OJ-style re-execution.
- Replay the agent's entries through a ledger.
- Report pass^k.

---


## 4. Gaps: under-served axes

1. Multi-period temporal state (the largest gap). No open benchmark has an agent run *N* consecutive accounting or FP&A periods where:
   - opening balances, accrual reversals, prepaid and deferred-revenue rollforwards, depreciation schedules and covenant tests depend on its own earlier outputs; and
   - new actuals and documents arrive each period.
   - What exists falls short in specific ways:
     - AccountingBench does this, but it is closed, uses one company and reports best-of-3.
     - APEX–Accounting is explicitly single-period ("frozen at month-end close").
     - SpreadsheetBench 2, BankerToolBench and FrontierFinance are single snapshots.
     - EnterpriseArena, CEO-Bench and Vending-Bench have state but no bookkeeping or document work.
   - Evidence that it matters:
     - AccountingBench: error compounding after month 4–5.
     - FinMaster: error propagation.
     - Finch: pass rate halves with more than 2 tasks.
     - Fin-RATE: −18.6% on longitudinal questions.
2. Recovery from errors and debugging inside a workflow.
   - SpreadsheetBench 2 debugging is the hardest category (≤12%), and BlueFin's validation and perturbation criteria are the lowest.
   - No benchmark *injects an error in period t and checks whether the agent detects and corrects it at t+k* (for example, a mis-coded expense discovered in a later flux review).
3. Messy, conflicting, multi-system real data. Most benchmarks use clean SEC filings or synthetic worlds; BankerToolBench names "standardized data sources rather than messy live-deal information" as a limitation. The exceptions:
   - Finch: Enron emails and spreadsheet version histories, 2000-era.
   - AccountingBench: real Stripe/Ramp/Mercury data, closed.
   - FinalityBench: faulted streams, but synthetic and narrow (payments).
   - Missing: late-arriving bank feeds, duplicate vendor records, scanned or partial invoices, FX-converted statements, mid-period chart-of-accounts changes and disagreements between subledger and GL.
4. Cross-document and cross-system reconciliation at scale.
   - Covered as single-period or single-bundle tasks: APEX–Accounting reconciliation (61 tasks), FinBalance and FinAuditing (XBRL).
   - Not covered: three-way match (PO / receipt / invoice), intercompany eliminations, subledger↔GL↔bank tie-outs across periods, and tracing a metric across board deck ↔ model ↔ GL. BankerToolBench found inconsistent LTM revenue between slides, which shows consistency across artifacts is a real failure mode that is rarely tested.
5. Jurisdictions and standards beyond US GAAP / SEC.
   - US-centric: Finance Agent Benchmark, FinanceBench, FinanceQA, BankerToolBench (stated), FrontierFinance, TaxCalcBench, FinTagging (US-GAAP taxonomy).
   - Exceptions: FinDeepResearch (8 markets, 4 languages), FinSearchComp/BizFinBench/FinGAIA (China), EDINET-Bench (Japan), SpreadsheetBench 2 (Indian data sources), Finch (UK PESA, Canadian public accounts).
   - Not covered: IFRS vs. US GAAP treatment differences (leases, development costs, revaluation), statutory local-GAAP reporting, VAT/GST, multi-currency consolidation, and non-English source documents in *operational* (not QA) tasks.
6. Private-company, SMB, government and non-profit finance.
   - Private companies appear only as synthetic worlds (APEX–Accounting, CFAgentBench) or a single real SaaS company (AccountingBench).
   - No government or fund accounting (encumbrances, budgetary vs. GAAP basis).
   - No non-profit restricted-fund accounting.
   - No real private-company management accounts.
7. Accounting operations vs. investment QA. The field is still weighted toward investment research QA and sell-side or IB deliverables. Thin or missing:
   - Consolidation, explicitly excluded by APEX–Accounting.
   - Tax provision (ASC 740 / IAS 12) and indirect tax.
   - Revenue recognition (ASC 606 / IFRS 15) with multi-element contracts.
   - Lease accounting (ASC 842 / IFRS 16).
   - Audit fieldwork: sampling, PBC lists, workpapers, tie-outs. Existing audit benchmarks only detect errors.
   - Treasury: 13-week cash forecasts, borrowing-base certificates.
8. Controls, approvals and human-in-the-loop.
   - Only CFAgentBench tests "pause before money movement".
   - TheAgentCompany and τ²-bench include asking a person.
   - EnterpriseBench has access control.
   - No finance benchmark tests segregation of duties, materiality thresholds, approval workflows or the audit trail.
9. Anti-gaming and integrity checks. Gaming is observed but rarely scored:
   - Fabricated reconciling items (AccountingBench).
   - Forced-balance plugs and hidden white-font rows (FrontierFinance).
   - Hardcoded outputs (MBABench, BlueFin, Finch).
   - "Reported ≠ entries" (FinBalance).
   - Plug detection, formula provenance, the ledger-replay gap and evidence citation should be first-class metrics.
10. Reliability and statistical power. Long-horizon finance benchmarks are tiny:
    - FrontierFinance 25 tasks, BankerToolBench 100, BlueFin 131, APEX–Accounting 160, AccountingBench 1 company.
    - Each run costs about $5–50 or more.
    - Pass^k is rarely reported, and where it is, it is very low (2.6% Pass^8).
    - A procedurally generated world with exact oracles (FinMaster, FinBalance and FinalityBench style), anchored in real data, could add volume without losing verifiability.
11. Verifiability of long trajectories. Benchmarks with deterministic checks tend to have weak finance depth (τ²-bench, AppWorld, Toolathlon); deep-finance benchmarks rely on LLM judges (FrontierFinance judge r = 0.63). Almost no benchmark combines deep finance, a long horizon and mostly deterministic grading. The closest are FinBalance and CFAgentBench, both synthetic and short.
12. Contamination and point-in-time.
    - Public 10-K-based items (FinanceQA on Costco, FinanceBench) are contaminated.
    - Live-data benchmarks drift.
    - BankerToolBench's offline historical anchoring and SEC-QA's refreshable generation are partial fixes. A Look-Ahead-Bench (arXiv 2601.13770 <https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13770>) style point-in-time discipline is rare in agentic finance tasks.

---


## 5. Finance workflow archetypes: coverage map

✅ = substantively covered · ◐ = partial (single period, QA-only or tiny) · ✗ = not found

| Archetype | Coverage | Where | Notes / gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3-statement model build | ✅ | FrontierFinance, MBABench, SpreadsheetBench 2, BankerToolBench | All single snapshot; none roll the model forward with new actuals or re-forecast |
| DCF | ✅ | BankerToolBench (18%), FrontierFinance, SpreadsheetBench 2, BlueFin, MBABench | Well covered; perturbation testing only in BlueFin |
| LBO / credit model | ✅ | BankerToolBench (15%), FrontierFinance, SpreadsheetBench 2, MBABench | |
| Merger model / accretion-dilution | ✅ | BankerToolBench, FrontierFinance, SpreadsheetBench 2 | Models weakest here (BankerToolBench) |
| Trading comps / precedents | ✅ | BankerToolBench (10%), SpreadsheetBench 2, Finance Agent v2 (unverified) | |
| Pitchbook / CIM / investment memo | ✅ | BankerToolBench, FrontierFinance (LBO memo), APEX-Agents | Rubric-judged only |
| Equity research report | ✅ | FinDeepResearch, FinResearchBench | LLM-judged |
| Filing QA / beat-miss / adjustments | ✅ | Finance Agent Benchmark, FinanceBench, FinanceQA, FinSearchComp | Saturating or contaminated |
| IPO / S-1 diligence | ◐ | IPO Finance Agent | One issuer public |
| Journal entries / data entry | ◐ | APEX–Accounting (26), FinBalance, FinMaster | Single period |
| Account / bank reconciliation | ◐ | APEX–Accounting (61), AccountingBench, FinBalance, CFAgentBench, FinalityBench | Multi-month bank rec with timing items: only AccountingBench (closed) |
| Schedules & accruals (prepaids, deferred revenue, fixed assets) | ◐ | APEX–Accounting (45), AccountingBench | No rollforward across periods in open benchmarks |
| Month-end close (full checklist, multi-period) | ◐/✗ | AccountingBench (closed); APEX–Accounting (single period) | Major gap |
| Variance / flux analysis, budget vs. actual | ◐ | APEX–Accounting (28), Finch (planning & budgeting) | No multi-period FP&A cycle (budget, then actuals, then reforecast) |
| Consolidation / intercompany / FX | ✗/◐ | SpreadsheetBench 2 (consolidation modeling); APEX–Accounting excludes it | Gap |
| Revenue recognition (606/15), leases (842/16) | ✗ | — | Gap |
| Tax (corporate provision, VAT/GST) | ✗/◐ | TaxCalcBench (US personal), TheAgentCompany Form 6765, SpreadsheetBench 2 (deferred tax) | Gap |
| Audit fieldwork / XBRL review | ◐ | FinAuditing, FinTagging, AuditBench, FinMaster auditing, EDINET fraud | Detection QA only; no sampling or workpapers |
| Covenant compliance certificate / borrowing base | ✗ | (FrontierFinance lender models; BankerToolBench LevFin indirectly) | Gap; a natural verifiable multi-period task |
| Credit memo / underwriting | ◐ | MortarBench (mortgage, exact match), CACE-Bench (synthetic credit pipeline; GitHub only) | No corporate credit memo benchmark |
| Treasury / cash forecasting (13-week) | ◐ | EnterpriseArena, CEO-Bench, Vending-Bench (implicit) | No document-grounded rolling cash forecast |
| AP / AR / collections, three-way match | ◐ | CFAgentBench, CARB (unverified), Finch (AP/AR 10 workflows) | |
| Payments operations | ◐ | DABStep, FinalityBench | |
| Portfolio rebalancing (drift bands, tax lots, constraints) | ✗/◐ | InvestorBench (single asset), BizFinBench v2 (online allocation), FinGAIA (weakest category), MCP-Universe | Gap for deterministic, constraint-checked rebalancing |
| Fund NAV / fund accounting / capital accounts / waterfall | ✗ | — (searches returned nothing) | Clear gap |
| Wealth management / advisory | ◐ | Wealth-mgmt TheAgentCompany extension (12 task pairs), GDPval advisors | |
| Loan origination | ◐ | MortarBench | |
| Regulatory / compliance actions | ◐ | FinToolBench, ReguSim (arXiv 2608.19974 <https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19974>), CFAgentBench approvals | |

---


## 6. Design implications for differentiating a new benchmark

These are suggestions drawn from the gaps above.

1. Make time the core axis. Use a persistent company world (or several) run for 6–24 monthly periods. Each period's close produces:
   - the ledger,
   - schedules (prepaids, deferred revenue, fixed assets, leases, debt),
   - reconciliations, and
   - an updated 3-statement model, cash forecast and covenant certificate.
   These outputs are the *inputs* to period t+1. Score per period so a long run gives a dense signal, and report both an "independent" score (oracle opening balances) and a "compounded" score (the agent's own balances). The difference between them measures error propagation.
2. Hidden canonical event log as the oracle (FinalityBench/FinBalance style). Generate the business's true transactions, then render them into messy, conflicting views: bank feed, PSP payouts, AP invoices (PDF and scans), payroll exports, emails and spreadsheet versions. Ground truth is exact; the agent sees realistic noise. Seed the world from real anonymized patterns (EnterpriseArena-style R→S) for realism.
3. Mostly deterministic grading. Use:
   - accounting identities (BS balances, cash ties to bank, subledger = GL control accounts);
   - ledger replay (reported vs. supported numbers);
   - perturbation re-execution of the agent's model (change an assumption and check that outputs move correctly);
   - covenant and ratio recomputation;
   - plug and hardcode detectors.
   Reserve rubric/LLM judging for narrative artifacts such as flux commentary and lender memos, and audit judge agreement.
4. Inject errors and late information. Plant misclassifications or missing accruals in early periods and surface evidence later; score detection and correction. Include restatement and true-up events.
5. Non-US and multi-standard variants. Use IFRS and a local-GAAP entity, multi-currency subsidiaries with consolidation and intercompany, and VAT/GST. Optionally include non-English source documents. Few existing benchmarks do this operationally.
6. Under-covered archetypes as first-class tasks: multi-period close, consolidation, covenant compliance certificate, 13-week cash forecast with variance to actuals, rolling reforecast, fund NAV / capital-account rollforward and waterfall, and constraint-checked portfolio rebalancing. All are naturally verifiable.
7. Controls and human-in-the-loop. Approval gates for payments and journal entries above materiality (CFAgentBench-style), segregation of duties, and a simulated controller or auditor to ask (TheAgentCompany / τ²-bench style). Score both inappropriate autonomy and over-asking.
8. Integrity metrics as headline numbers: fabricated-evidence rate, unsupported-number rate, plug usage, and citation of source documents (FinBalance found citation pressure barely helped).
9. Reliability and cost: report pass^k per period, tokens, cost and wall-clock time. Release an offline, date-anchored, containerized environment (Harbor/Inspect compatible, as BankerToolBench and APEX-Agents are) with a held-out private world set to limit contamination.

---


====================================================================================================
4. TOP 10 DATA SOURCES (ranked by priority to build)
====================================================================================================

Caveats that apply to all ten
  - Only 5 of 10 are pulled and validated (#1, #3, #4, #6, #7). #2 profiled only, #5 fund-level only, #9 schema only, #8 and #10 not pulled.
  - Most primary sites were blocked in the sandbox; pulls used mirrors (public S3, Kaggle, GitHub, HF datasets-server).
  - Mirrors are third-party: some state no licence, completeness varies; re-check against the primary source before release.
  - Archive snapshots are frozen (2020 PDF crawl, 2024-25 Data.gov archive, old Kaggle copies): fine for dev; test splits need live pulls. #3, #5, #6, #7, #9, #10 refresh monthly or quarterly.
  - MSRB EMMA and Chapter 11 claims agents restrict scraping/redistribution: hand-collect. Freddie Mac/Fannie Mae loan files, FINRA TRACE and NAIC statements need paid licences and are excluded.
  - Source totals grade numbers only; memos and judgment calls need a rubric or LLM judge.
  - Some details come from web search, not direct access (marked 'unverified' in docs/sources/).


#1 Municipal annual reports and bond documents (ACFRs, budget books, single audits, actuarial valuations, official statements)
  links:
    - GovText / End-of-Term 2020 PDF archive (S3, CC0): https://s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/us-west-2.opendata.source.coop/govscape/eota-ocr/README.md
    - GovScape search over the same PDFs: https://govscape.net
    - MSRB EMMA (browse only; ToS forbid scraping): https://emma.msrb.org
    - Our index of finance PDFs in the archive: samples/govtext_finance_pdf_index.parquet
  pulled: VALIDATED. Austin, 17 ACFR PDFs FY2001-2019: statement located 15/16; fund balance ties 15/15 within year, 11/12 across years (the break is FY2011's restated opening balance, +$19.3M, Note 16). Archive index: 6,203 ACFRs, 3,394 budget books, 872 single audits, 433 actuarial valuations, 167 official statements (2020 crawl, PDF + OCR).
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - Municipal credit review (muni credit / rating analyst): extract 5-10 yrs of fund statements [GT] -> normalise restatements and special items -> fund balance and liquidity trend -> debt schedule from the official statement tied to the debt note [GT] -> coverage under the bond resolution's revenue definition -> pension liability roll-forward [GT] -> peer percentile and rating memo
    - Annual surveillance update: add the new annual report -> roll every metric forward [GT] -> explain changes -> revise the rating view
  caveats:
    - Archive PDFs are from the 2020 crawl, so mostly FY2001-FY2019
    - EMMA prohibits scraping; get official statements from issuer websites
    - OCR quality varies; PDF text extraction splits digits ('$ 1 30,163')

#2 State and city ledgers and checkbooks
  links:
    - Harvard LIL Data.gov archive (Source Cooperative): https://source.coop/harvard-lil/gov-data
    - Archive search: https://lil.law.harvard.edu/data-gov-archive
    - S3 paths: https://s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/us-west-2.opendata.source.coop/harvard-lil/gov-data/ (metadata/data_gov/<slug>/v1.json, collections/data_gov/<slug>/v1.zip)
    - Live example: https://data.cityofchicago.org ; Kaggle mirror chicago/chicago-payments: https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/chicago/chicago-payments
    - Slugs: general-ledger (Oklahoma GL FY22-FY25 Q1, ~2 GB), state-of-oklahoma-vendor-payments-fiscal-year-2019-6704f, austin-finance-online-echeckbook (642 MB), state-of-iowa-checkbook (6 GB), citywide-payroll-data-fiscal-year (NYC), payments (Chicago); CA State Controller returns FY2003-FY2023
  pulled: PROFILED. Chicago payments (Kaggle 2019 snapshot): 829,738 vouchers, $97.5B, 53 departments, 342k vendors, 75k contracts; 18.5% of rows dated by year only, 78% missing department, 97,974 same vendor/amount/date duplicate candidates.
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - Year-end close review (state/city comptroller analyst): trial balance by fund from the ledger [GT] -> map accounts to statement lines -> vendor payments vs ledger spending by agency -> reconcile to audited fund statements [GT] -> budget-to-actual variances -> commentary
    - Payment-integrity audit (inspector general / internal auditor): de-duplicate vendor master -> duplicate and split payments -> payments without contracts -> digit tests -> sample to budget lines -> exception report
  caveats:
    - Snapshots date from 2023-25; match ledger years to the ACFR year
    - Duplicate labels have no ground truth; need experts
    - Schemas drift across years; Oklahoma bag is CC-BY

#3 Auto-loan and commercial-mortgage securitizations (SEC Reg AB II: ABS-EE loan tapes + 10-D servicer reports + 424B prospectus waterfalls)
  links:
    - SEC Reg AB II asset-level data: https://www.sec.gov/oit/announcement/regabii-asset-level-requirements-compliance.html
    - EDGAR full-text search: https://www.sec.gov/edgar/search/
    - Kaggle mirror (World Omni auto trusts, 1 GB parquet): https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/denytran/10-year-automotive-loans-abs-ee
    - Hugging Face per-trust mirrors (1,072 repos, e.g. DenyTranDFW/AmeriCredit_Automobile_Receivables_Trust_2020_1_1801390): https://huggingface.co/DenyTranDFW
    - CMBS deal index (Kaggle): https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/cmdrvl/cmbs-deals-edgar-cik-and-collateral-distribution
  pulled: VALIDATED. 35,676,547 loan-months, 1,148,191 loans, Feb 2017-Mar 2024. Loan balance identity (begin - principal - charge-off - other adj = end) ties 100%; month-to-month continuity 99.62% (129k breaks > $100, 55.7k reporting gaps). Annualised net loss ~0.4-0.7%, 30+ DPD ~2.2-2.6%.
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - 24-month surveillance desk (structured-credit analyst): cut pool by FICO/term/state [GT] -> delinquency roll rates -> prepay/default/severity speeds -> pool roll-forward vs servicer report [GT] -> replicate waterfall and trigger tests from the prospectus [GT] -> carry cumulative loss -> monthly note
    - New-issue check: tape at issuance vs prospectus strat tables [GT] -> flag differences -> price-sensitivity view
  caveats:
    - No registered RMBS since 2014: coverage is auto loans/leases and CMBS
    - 10-D and 424B are not mirrored; need EDGAR
    - Kaggle mirror pools several trusts without a deal ID; HF per-trust repos hold 7 to 60 filings
    - Trap: repossession proceeds are a subset of recoveries (double count -> negative losses)

#4 Utility regulatory accounts (FERC Form 1/2/6/60, EQR wholesale transactions, RUS co-ops) via Catalyst PUDL
  links:
    - PUDL documentation: https://docs.catalyst.coop/pudl/en/stable/
    - PUDL data bucket (S3, CC BY 4.0): https://s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/pudl.catalyst.coop/?list-type=2&prefix=stable/&delimiter=/
    - FERC eCollection (primary filings): https://ecollection.ferc.gov
  pulled: VALIDATED. 375 utilities, 1994-2025. Plant-in-service roll-forward ties 99.72% within year (226,738 rows) and 98.82% across years (211,729 pairs; 1,849 real restatements/reclassifications). Idaho Power: 32 years, 100% / 99.7%.
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - Rate case revenue requirement (utility/commission regulatory analyst): rate-base roll-forward from plant and depreciation [GT] -> cost of capital -> revenue requirement vs PUDL's published rate-base table [GT] -> test-year normalisation -> peer O&M benchmarking -> testimony summary
    - Purchased-power reconciliation (utility accountant): buyer's purchased-power schedule vs sellers' EQR transactions [GT] -> explain differences
  caveats:
    - PUDL has cleaned the data; use raw FERC XBRL/DBF when acquisition is part of the task
    - Trap: retirements stored as negatives (textbook sign ties only 43%)
    - CC BY 4.0 requires attribution; EQR is 125 GB

#5 US fund holdings (SEC N-PORT + 13F)
  links:
    - SEC N-PORT data sets: https://www.sec.gov/data-research/sec-markets-data/form-n-port-data-sets
    - Hugging Face mirror (CC0, all SEC tables): https://huggingface.co/datasets/trader298/sec-nport
    - Curated holdings: https://huggingface.co/datasets/ZipLime/fund-holdings-nport
    - 13F mirror: https://huggingface.co/datasets/ZipLime/institutional-portfolio-13f
  pulled: PARTIAL (fund level). 160 target-date-2050 reports across 8 series via HF datasets-server /filter; total assets - liabilities = net assets on 100/100 reports. Real event: Vanguard Target Retirement 2050 jumps ~$22B -> ~$54B in 2022 (Institutional series merged in). Holdings not pulled (HF CDN blocked; 6 GB index still warming).
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - Target-date fund due diligence (OCIO manager-research analyst): look-through to underlying funds [GT] -> chain returns vs reported [GT] -> drift vs glide path -> attribution -> fee drag -> explain the merger break -> recommendation
    - Bond-fund liquidity stress (fund liquidity risk manager): classify holdings -> concentration -> duration vs reported rate risk [GT] -> redemption stress -> sale plan
  caveats:
    - Public data covers one month per fiscal quarter with ~60-day lag; broader monthly publication postponed (verify)
    - Placeholder CUSIPs and one row per identifier double-count if joined naively
    - HF file CDN blocked in the sandbox

#6 India mutual-fund portfolios (SEBI monthly portfolio workbooks per fund house + AMFI NAV/TER)
  links:
    - AMFI: https://www.amfiindia.com
    - Raw workbook mirror (GitHub, Aug-2026, 13 AMCs): https://github.com/subscriptionmanager26-png/fund-disclosures
    - Parsed holdings mirror (GitHub): https://github.com/kushagra-agarwal-a/fund-holdings-data
    - NAV history (Kaggle): https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/tharunreddy2911/mutual-fund-historic-nav-data
    - TER (Kaggle): https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/neeeeeemo/indian-mutual-funds-ter-total-expense-ratios
  pulled: VALIDATED. 13 fund houses, 715 schemes (31-Aug-2026). Holdings' implied net assets tie to the Grand Total for 99.2% of schemes; per-holding consistency 80% (rest is 2-decimal rounding on small positions). Header row anywhere from 2-12; ISIN column 0/2/3/7; % vs fraction (SBI uses both); Rs lakh; zips; UTI defeats the heuristic.
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - Quarterly client review and rebalance (mutual-fund distributor / adviser): parse six fund houses' workbooks [GT] -> look-through by stock/sector/size -> overlap -> SEBI category-rule check -> returns and volatility from NAV -> regular->direct switch via TER -> tax-aware trade list; repeat monthly
    - Debt-fund credit and duration monitor (treasury investor): fortnightly holdings -> rating migration -> issuer concentration -> recompute yield/duration vs reported [GT] -> exits
  caveats:
    - Raw mirror holds one month; more months mean scraping 40+ fund-house sites
    - No explicit licence (statutory disclosures)
    - Layouts change without notice

#7 Nonprofit tax returns (IRS 990 / 990-PF e-file XML, 2009-2026)
  links:
    - IRS Form 990 series downloads: https://www.irs.gov/node/70096
    - GivingTuesday data lake (public S3): https://gt990datalake-rawdata.s3.amazonaws.com/?list-type=2&delimiter=/
    - Index parquet (range-readable): https://gt990datalake-rawdata.s3.amazonaws.com/Indices/990xmls/index_all_years_efiledata_xmls_created_on_2024-07-23.parquet
    - Raw XML: https://gt990datalake-rawdata.s3.amazonaws.com/EfileData/XmlFiles/<ObjectId>_public.xml
    - Parsed (HF, CC0): https://huggingface.co/datasets/piercewetter3/irs-990-parsed
  pulled: VALIDATED. Wikimedia Foundation 2009-2022 (14 returns, 13 schema versions): Part XI net-asset reconciliation 10/10, roll-forward EOY(t)=BOY(t+1) 13/13, functional expenses 14/14. Index: 6,245,445 returns.
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - Foundation year-end close and payout plan (foundation CFO / tax preparer): classify ~4,000 sale lots ST/LT [GT] -> reconcile lot gains to reported gain -> excise tax -> minimum distributable amount [GT] -> map holdings to sectors -> tax-lot sale plan to policy
    - Grant due diligence (program officer / charity regulator): 10 years of financials [GT] -> program ratio, reserves, revenue concentration -> endowment analysis -> single-audit cross-check
  caveats:
    - Schema changes nearly every year; element names repeat in schedules
    - Lot detail varies by preparer; may not sum to reported gain
    - Single-audit data (FAC) needs an API key

#8 US Chapter 11 case dockets (DIP budgets/variance reports, monthly operating reports, claims registers, disclosure statements)
  links:
    - CourtListener RECAP archive: https://www.courtlistener.com/recap/
    - Claims agent (Kroll): https://restructuring.ra.kroll.com
    - Claims agent (Stretto): https://cases.stretto.com
    - Text-only mirror (Delaware bankruptcy court): https://huggingface.co/datasets/alea-institute/kl3m-data-pacer-deb
    - Post-confirmation trusts on EDGAR: Lehman Brothers Holdings Plan Trust (CIK 806085), Motors Liquidation GUC Trust (CIK 40730)
  pulled: NOT PULLED (hosts blocked; no bulk mirror of MORs or claims registers).
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - Creditor-committee monitoring (restructuring financial advisor): read scanned monthly reports; cash roll-forward [GT] -> cumulative budget variance vs tolerance [GT] -> liquidity runway -> fees vs budget -> claims by class [GT] -> recovery waterfall vs disclosure statement [GT] -> committee update
    - Recovery analysis (distressed investor): claims register and plan [GT] -> recovery per class -> sensitivity to asset values
  caveats:
    - Hand-collect 5-10 cases
    - Claims-agent terms vary on redistribution
    - RECAP holds only what users bought; coverage varies by court

#9 FFIEC bank Call Reports + UBPR (+ FDIC failures)
  links:
    - FFIEC Central Data Repository: https://cdr.ffiec.gov/public/
    - Call Report mirror (CC0, 2001-2026): https://huggingface.co/datasets/kairusama/call-report-one
    - UBPR mirror (CC0): https://huggingface.co/datasets/kairusama/ubpr-one
    - Bank ratios (Kaggle, CC0): https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/neutrino404/all-us-banks-financial-quarterly-ratios-2001-2023
  pulled: SCHEMA ONLY. 671,000 bank-quarters, 224 income/supplemental columns, 1,442 canonical columns.
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - Bank-failure post-mortem / exam prep (bank examiner): 20 quarters -> capital and liquidity -> allowance roll-forward [GT] -> margin bridge -> real-estate concentration -> recompute ratios vs UBPR [GT] -> exam memo
    - Early-warning screen (bank credit analyst): screen the universe -> back-test against later failures [GT] -> watchlist
  caveats:
    - Structured data: little raw-file mess; pair with bank PDFs
    - Field codes and schedules changed over 25 years
    - HF CDN blocked in the sandbox

#10 Private credit: BDC schedules of investments + credit agreements (EX-10)
  links:
    - SEC BDC data sets (monthly, from XBRL; launched June 2025): https://www.sec.gov/data-research/sec-markets-data/bdc-data-sets
    - Credit agreements (HF, 2001-2024): https://huggingface.co/datasets/yonathanarbel/SEC_Exhibit_10
    - Scanned exhibits (HF): https://huggingface.co/datasets/arthrod/sec-ex10-exhibits
  pulled: NOT PULLED (mirrors identified).
  workflows ([GT] = checkable against a number the source reports):
    - Private-credit surveillance (private-credit analyst): match borrowers across BDCs -> mark dispersion -> schedule total vs balance sheet [GT] -> covenant test using the agreement's EBITDA definition -> watchlist
    - Covenant compliance certificate (lender / borrower CFO): extract covenant definitions -> compute leverage and coverage with add-backs -> compare to thresholds each quarter
  caveats:
    - Data sets start June 2025; earlier history from filings
    - SEC doesn't guarantee accuracy; XBRL tagging errors
    - Covenant ground truth partial (compliance certificates rarely filed)


====================================================================================================
5. OTHER SOURCES, MIRRORS AND THE AVOID LIST
====================================================================================================

## 0. TL;DR

Where the whitespace is. Existing finance benchmarks are dominated by (a) QA over US 10-Ks
(FinanceBench, Finance Agent, FinSearchComp) and (b) single-snapshot modeling deliverables
(SpreadsheetBench 2, BankerToolBench, FrontierFinance). The survey found no open benchmark with
multi-period state on real data, and none touching structured credit surveillance, municipal/
government accounts, utility regulatory accounting, fund holdings (N-PORT), nonprofit returns,
Chapter 11 case work, or non-US fund disclosures. Those are the areas below.

Selection principle. Prefer sources where the source itself reports totals that bottom-up
work must reproduce (natural ground truth). That is what keeps a long, messy, real trajectory
gradeable. In the six PoCs with an identity check, the source's own identities tie for 92–100% of
records once conventions are handled (e.g., FERC roll-forward 99.7%, ABS-EE loan identity 100%,
India Grand Total 99.2%, Austin cross-year 11/12), and the *exceptions* are real restatements,
mergers and special items: exactly the judgment points that separate strong models.

### Ranked shortlist

Scores 1–5. D concept depth · T temporal depth / refresh · M messiness (raw-to-workbook
burden) · G natural ground truth · X differentiation · S scrapability + licence.

| # | Source (env) | D | T | M | G | X | S | PoC in sandbox |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Municipal finance pack: ACFR PDFs + checkbook/GL + official statements + actuarial valuations | 5 | 4 | 5 | 5 | 5 | 3 | ✅ Austin ACFR 17 yrs (PDF→panel, 15/15 ties); Chicago checkbook |
| 2 | SEC Reg AB II ABS-EE loan tapes + 10-D servicer reports + 424B waterfalls (auto, CMBS) | 5 | 5 | 4 | 5 | 5 | 4 | ✅ 35.7M loan-months, 100% loan identity |
| 3 | FERC Form 1/2/6/60 + EQR + RUS 7/12 utility regulatory accounting (PUDL) | 5 | 5 | 3 | 5 | 5 | 5 | ✅ 375 utilities × 32 yrs, 99.7% roll-forward ties |
| 4 | US fund stack: N-PORT (+13F, N-CSR, prospectus fees, Fama-French) | 5 | 4 | 3 | 5 | 5 | 3 | ◐ fund-level pulled (A−L=NA 100%); holdings index warming |
| 5 | India AMC monthly portfolio workbooks + AMFI NAV/TER | 4 | 5 | 5 | 4 | 5 | 3 | ✅ 13 AMCs, 715 schemes, 99.2% tie to Grand Total |
| 6 | IRS 990 / 990-PF e-file XML (+ Federal Audit Clearinghouse, USAspending) | 4 | 5 | 4 | 5 | 5 | 5 | ✅ 14 yrs Wikimedia, all identities tie |
| 7 | US Chapter 11 dockets: first-day decl., DIP budgets/variance, scanned MORs, claims registers, disclosure-statement waterfalls | 5 | 3 | 5 | 4 | 5 | 2 | ✗ (claims-agent sites blocked; no mirror of MORs) |
| 8 | FFIEC Call Reports + UBPR (+ FDIC failures) | 4 | 5 | 3 | 5 | 4 | 4 | ◐ schema verified (CC0 mirror, 2001–2026) |
| 9 | BDC schedules of investments + EX-10 credit agreements (+ N-PORT loan holdings) | 5 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 5 | 3 | — (mirrors identified) |
| 10 | UK Companies House accounts (iXBRL, 33.5M company-periods, first-reported vs. latest) | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 5 | 4 | — (Kaggle mirror identified) |
| 11 | Public-pension PE fund reports (WSIB PDFs) + Public Plans Data | 4 | 4 | 5 | 4 | 5 | 3 | — (survey agent verified identities, IRR within 17 bp) |
| 12 | UK council "spend over £500" + RO outturn + Statements of Accounts | 4 | 5 | 5 | 3 | 5 | 3 | — (Devon 2011–2026 on GitHub) |

Tier 3 (useful supplements or answer keys): CA State Controller FTR and France DGFiP trial
balances (government-published ratios as answer keys), Pillar 3 / EBA Data Hub, Japan EDINET
raw XBRL, Brazil CVM funds, Enron spreadsheets (already used by Finch), Treasury DTS→MTS, FRED/
ALFRED vintages, Ken French factors.

Recommended flagship environments to deepen first (§3): F1 municipal credit review,
F2 ABS surveillance desk, F3 portfolio rebalance (US TDF and India wealth desk), and
F4 utility rate case. Together they cover four personas, three jurisdictions' worth of
messiness, and the strongest natural ground truth.

### 2.9 Tier 2 in brief
| Source | Why | Main caveat |
|---|---|---|
| BDC schedules of investments + EX-10 credit agreements (HF yonathanarbel/SEC_Exhibit_10, arthrod/sec-ex10-exhibits incl. scanned page images) | private-credit marks across holders, covenant definitions buried in legal text (C5) | SEC BDC Data Sets unmirrored; XBRL tagging errors |
| UK Companies House accounts (Kaggle thomassimm/uk-company-accounts-wide-2014-2026, OGL) | 33.5M SMB company-periods, UK GAAP, first-reported vs. latest (9.36% restated) | mirror is parsed facts; raw iXBRL needs Companies House |
| WSIB private-equity quarterly PDFs (Kaggle, original PDFs + OCR) + Public Plans Data | CIO-office PE pacing: cash flows from cumulative deltas, IRR (recomputed within 17 bp), PME | mirror stops 2021Q2; newer quarters on sib.wa.gov |
| UK council monthly payments > £500 (Devon on GitHub 2011–2026) + RO outturn + accounts | non-US government, very messy CSVs, monthly refresh | needs reconciling-item judgment; RO pulls blocked here |
| CA SCO FTR, France DGFiP balances, NY OSC FSMS | government-published ratios and stress scores = free answer keys over hundreds of entities | French data behind HF CDN |
| Pillar 3 / EBA Pillar 3 Data Hub | EU/UK bank templates with many reconciliations | QA benchmark exists (arXiv 2609.03654); no mirror |
| Japan EDINET raw XBRL (Kaggle sirokujira/edinet20xx) / Korea DART | non-US GAAP/IFRS with calc linkbases and audit reports | language; DART behind HF CDN |
| Enron spreadsheets + emails | only large corpus of real working finance-ops workbooks | Finch already built 172 workflows on it; differentiate by desk and chain length |


### 2.10 Avoid / deprioritise
- Licence: Freddie Mac / Fannie Mae loan-level (redistribution needs paid licence), FINRA TRACE
  history (paid), NAIC statements (paid), SEDAR+ and MSRB EMMA (ToS forbid scraping; hand-curate
  from issuers instead), Yahoo-derived prices.
- Low differentiation: plain 10-K/10-Q QA, LendingClub (FinBen/CALM), synthetic ledgers.

## 25. Exact reachable URLs and IDs (copy-paste)


## 2. RANKED TOP-5 FOR THIS SLICE

Scores are 1–5 on Workflow depth, Realness/messiness, Verifiability, Differentiation and Scrapability (from this container).

1. PUDL utility-regulatory accounting universe: FERC Form 1/2/6/60 + EQR transactions + RUS 7/12 + SEC Ex.21 linkage. (D5 R5 V5 Df5 S5)
   USofA account-level statements are among the most granular public accounting data anywhere (1994–2025, about 385 Form 1 respondents; 613 co-ops). They come with XBRL calc rules and weights, debit/credit attributes, cross-schedule tie-outs, a published rate-base table usable as a reference answer, and budget columns (RUS 7).
   EQR adds 125 GB of real transaction-level sales that reconcile (imperfectly, realistically) to buyers' Form 1 purchased-power schedules. That covers both "universe-broad" and "one entity deep".
   No LLM benchmark uses it. It is the only large source fully downloadable in-container (public S3, CC BY 4.0), plus Kaggle.
2. Enron spreadsheet and email corpus (HF enronarchive/mail with original filenames and email index; GitHub SheetJS/enron_xls; HF intellekthq/enron-ferc-pst). (D5 R5 V4 Df3 S3)
   The only large corpus of *real* working finance-ops spreadsheets: desk P&L, positions, capital-book roll-forwards, tax reserves, gas volume accounting, plans. Daily version series and email threads give before/after ground truth.
   Differentiation is reduced because Finch already uses it. Avoid by targeting other custodians and desks (logistics, tax, origination), longer chains, and joins to external real data (FERC Form 1 or EQR context).
   Access: git clone works. HF large files need CDN egress.
3. UK Companies House accounts (iXBRL), Kaggle thomassimm/uk-company-accounts-wide-2014-2026. (D4 R5 V4 Df5 S4)
   Private and small-company (SMB) financials at national scale (33.5M company-periods). Plenty of genuine mess: filleted accounts, unit and tagging anomalies, and a 9.36% restatement rate with first-reported vs latest vintages built in. Balance-sheet identities serve as checks.
   UK GAAP (FRS 102/105) gives a non-US jurisdiction. No benchmark found.
   Caveat: the mirror is parsed facts only. Raw iXBRL documents (for document-reading tasks) require Companies House access from outside the container.
4. SEC structured disclosures beyond 10-K: FSN notes and dimensions + calc linkbase, companyfacts as-filed vintages, 8-K earnings releases, DEF 14A pay-versus-performance (ECD), N-PORT and 13F. (D5 R4 V5 Df3 S3)
   Supports segment (ASU 2023-07), restatement-forensics, PvP-recompute and fund look-through workflows with machine-checkable answers (calc linkbase, net-asset identities, TSR recompute).
   Differentiation is moderate (SEC is crowded) but strong if tasks avoid 10-K QA.
   Kaggle CC0 mirrors cover companyfacts (2009+). The full FSN history with cal/dim and EX-99.1 exhibits need a SEC fetch outside the container. HF N-PORT and robosystems need CDN egress.
5. Government AP, vendor-payment and P-card ledgers (Kaggle san-francisco/sf-vendor-payments vouchers + PO summary, tfshiloh/csv-version-missouri-checkbook-fy2010fy2017, chicago/chicago-payments, Oklahoma P-card, Canada Receiver General; HF USAspending). (D4 R5 V3 Df5 S5)
   The rarest category, real transactional AP, and it is in-container downloadable. It supports audit, controls, close and cut-off workflows (three-way-match style PO-vs-voucher checks, duplicates, splits, vendor master).
   Verifiability is weaker (PO-vs-voucher sums are natural, but anomaly labels need experts and there are no ACFR tie-outs in the mirrors).

Honorable mentions:
- Japan EDINET raw XBRL (Kaggle sirokujira/edinet20xx, with _cal.xml and AuditDoc/KAMs; PDL 1.0).
- Korea DART (HF eddmpython/dartlab-data, CC BY 4.0, needs CDN egress).
- IRS 990 (HF piercewetter3/irs-990-parsed, CC0, strong internal arithmetic).
- Berka real bank ledger (Kaggle, in-container).
- IDX Excel statements (spreadsheet-native disclosures).

Avoid or deprioritize:
- SEDAR+ (ToS bans scraping).
- NAIC (paid).
- FINRA short interest, Yahoo-derived prices, Fannie/Freddie loan files (redistribution terms).
- Scraped transcripts (provenance).
- Synthetic ledgers (mindweave, IBM AML, APEX worlds).

7. PROOF-OF-CONCEPT PULLS (docs/04_poc_results.md)

# Proof-of-concept pulls: what we actually fetched and verified

All pulls ran inside the sandbox on 2026-10-17. Network constraint: the sandbox's egress policy
blocks the primary government hosts (sec.gov, data.sec.gov, efts.sec.gov, emma.msrb.org,
api.usaspending.gov, banks.data.fdic.gov, ffiec.gov, fred.stlouisfed.org,
fiscaldata.treasury.gov, amfiindia.com, bseindia.com, nseindia.com,
Companies House, filings.xbrl.org, ...) and the Hugging Face file CDN (*.cdn.hf.co,
*.xethub.hf.co). Reachable: huggingface.co API, datasets-server.huggingface.co,
raw.githubusercontent.com/GitHub, public S3 buckets, kaggle.com, arxiv.org.

So each PoC uses the best reachable path. The "primary path" column says how a production
scraper would fetch it once those hosts are allowlisted (see
https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments#network-access).

| # | Source | Reachable path used | Primary path (production) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | FERC Form 1 (utility regulatory accounts) | Catalyst PUDL public S3 pudl.catalyst.coop/stable/*.parquet | FERC eCollection XBRL (2021+) / VFP DBF (1994–2020) | ✅ pulled + validated |
| 2 | IRS Form 990 e-file XML (nonprofits) | GivingTuesday public S3 gt990datalake-rawdata (Parquet index via HTTP range + raw XML) | IRS bulk XML zips | ✅ pulled + validated |
| 3 | SEC Form N-PORT (monthly fund holdings) | HF mirror trader298/sec-nport (CC0) via datasets-server /filter | SEC N-PORT quarterly data sets | ⏳ server-side index warming; schema verified |
| 4 | FFIEC Call Reports (banks, 2001–2026 quarterly) | HF mirror kairusama/call-report-one (CC0) via datasets-server | FFIEC CDR bulk | ⏳ schema verified (224–1,442 cols) |
| 5 | SEC Form ABS-EE auto-loan tapes (World Omni trusts 2017–2024) | Kaggle denytran/10-year-automotive-loans-abs-ee (anonymous download, 1 GB Parquet) | EDGAR ABS-EE EX-102 XML + 10-D | ✅ pulled + validated |
| 6 | City of Chicago vendor payments (checkbook) | Kaggle chicago/chicago-payments (2019 snapshot) | Socrata data.cityofchicago.org (weekly refresh) | ✅ pulled + profiled |
| 7 | India AMC monthly portfolio disclosures (SEBI-mandated, Aug-2026) | GitHub subscriptionmanager26-png/fund-disclosures (sparse clone) | Each AMC's website (manifests record source URLs) | ✅ pulled + parsed + validated |
| 8 | Municipal ACFR PDFs (City of Austin FY2001–FY2019) | End-of-Term 2020 web archive on public S3 (govscape/eota-ocr, CC0, PDFs + OCR) | Issuer websites / EMMA (EMMA ToS forbid scraping) | ✅ pulled + parsed + validated |

---

## PoC 1: FERC Form 1 via PUDL (poc/pudl_ferc1.py)

Fetched: schedules 204 (plant in service), 110 (balance sheet), 114 (income statement),
118 (retained earnings), 219 (accumulated depreciation changes), 375 utilities, 1994–2025.
Sample env seed for Idaho Power Company (32 years) in
samples/ferc1_idaho_power/ferc1_env_seed.xlsx.

Natural ground truth tested (plant-in-service roll-forward):

| Check | Universe (375 utilities) | Idaho Power |
|---|---|---|
| Within-year: begin + additions + retirements + adjustments + transfers = end | 99.72% of 226,738 rows tie | 100% of 1,762 |
| Across-year: end(t) = begin(t+1) | 98.82% of 211,729 pairs tie | 99.7%; 4 breaks > $1k |

Trap observed (real, not planted): FERC reports *retirements as negative numbers*. With the
"textbook" sign (− retirements) only 43% of rows tie. That is exactly the kind of convention a
model must infer from the data, and the ~1,850 cross-year breaks are real restatements and
reclassifications that a strong agent should find and explain.

Workflow scope: rate-base roll-forward → depreciation reserve → revenue requirement → peer
O&M benchmarking; 30 years × 375 utilities supports both deep and broad envs. Joins to EIA
(plant-level), SEC 10-K (parent holding companies, via PUDL's core_sec10k__* association tables).

## PoC 2: IRS Form 990 via GivingTuesday data lake (poc/irs990.py)

Fetched: Parquet index (6,245,445 returns; we read only 8 columns via HTTP range requests,
no full download) → all Form 990 returns for Wikimedia Foundation (EIN 20-0049703),
tax years 2009–2022 → raw XML (in samples/irs990_wikimedia/xml/) → parsed panel
irs990_env_seed.xlsx.

Natural ground truth tested:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Part XI net-asset reconciliation (lines 3–9 = EOY − BOY), 2013+ schema | 10/10 years tie within $1 |
| Net-asset roll-forward EOY(t) = BOY(t+1) | 13/13 year pairs tie within $1 |
| Part IX functional expenses: program + management + fundraising = total | 14/14 |

Traps hit while building the PoC (the same ones an agent hits):
1. *Same element names recur in schedules.* Grabbing the first match in the document picked up a
   Schedule value and broke the reconciliation. The parse must be scoped to the main form.
2. *Schema drift.* 14 returns span 13 schema versions; the IRS renamed elements in 2013
   (TotalRevenueCurrentYear → CYTotalRevenueAmt), and Part XI reconciliation does not exist
   before 2013. A naive parser silently returns NaN (and a naive check counts NaN as a tie).
3. *Sign ambiguity.* Part XI line 7 (investment expenses) is entered with inconsistent signs
   across filers.

Workflow scope: nonprofit financial health review over 10+ years (program ratio, months of
reserves, revenue concentration, executive comp from Schedule J, grants paid from Schedule I),
universe screens across 6M returns, and joins to Single Audit (Federal Audit Clearinghouse, by EIN)
and to grant-maker 990-PF Schedule I (grants paid ↔ grants received).

## PoC 5: ABS-EE auto-loan tapes (poc/absee_auto.py)

Fetched: 35,676,547 loan-month records, 1,148,191 loans, 86 monthly periods
(2017-02 → 2024-03) from World Omni Auto Receivables trusts' EX-102 asset-level exhibits.
Env seed samples/absee_world_omni/absee_env_seed.xlsx = bottom-up monthly pool surveillance
table + full monthly history of 300 sampled loans.

Natural ground truth tested:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Loan-level: begin − principal collected − charged-off − other adj. = end | 100.0% of 35.7M loan-months tie to the cent |
| Continuity: end(t) = begin(t+1), same asset | 99.62% of 34.5M pairs; 129k breaks > $100; 55.7k reporting gaps > 1 month |
| Pool level vs. Form 10-D servicer report | needs EDGAR (blocked here); 10-D reports pool balance, delinquencies, cumulative net loss, so the bottom-up table is checkable |

Traps hit (real data, not planted):
1. repossessedProceedsAmount is a *component* of recoveredAmount (~80% of it). Summing both
   double-counts recoveries and makes net losses negative. Correct net-loss rate ≈ 0.4–0.7%
   annualized (plausible for a prime auto book); 30+ DPD ≈ 2.2–2.6%.
2. 28 records carry a 1900-01-01 null-date sentinel as the reporting period.
3. Loans drop out and reappear (55.7k gaps), and balances jump on 129k transitions. These are
   real servicing events (repurchases, modifications, trust-level reporting gaps) that a
   surveillance analyst must explain, not smooth over.
4. The Kaggle mirror pools several trusts without a deal identifier; per-deal envs should use the
   per-trust HF repos (DenyTranDFW/<Trust_Name>_<CIK>, 1,072 repos) or EDGAR directly.

Workflow scope: monthly surveillance roll-forward (strats → roll rates → CPR/CDR/severity →
reconcile to 10-D → waterfall & trigger tests from the 424B prospectus → note), repeated for
36–80 months per trust, across ~650 auto trusts (+ ~375 CMBS trusts with property-level data).

## PoC 6: City of Chicago vendor payments (checkbook)

Fetched: 829,738 payment vouchers, $97.5B, 53 departments, 342k vendors, 75k contracts
(Kaggle snapshot, Dec 2019; the live Socrata dataset refreshes weekly).

Messiness observed: pre-2018 payments are rolled up to a bare year ("2013") instead of a
check date (18.5% of rows); department is missing on 78% of rows (direct vouchers, DV, are 71%);
97,974 rows share vendor + amount + date with another row (duplicate-payment *candidates*, most
of which are legitimate, so separating them is real audit judgment).

Workflow scope: fiscal-year budget-to-actual and duplicate-payment testing, reconciled to the
city's ACFR and annual appropriation ordinance (PDFs, not reachable from the sandbox).

## PoC 7: India AMC monthly portfolio workbooks (poc/india_amc_portfolios.py)

Fetched: raw month-end (31-Aug-2026) portfolio workbooks of 13 Indian AMCs (SBI, ICICI Pru,
DSP, Mirae, Franklin, Baroda BNP, UTI, PGIM, Union, Navi, JM, Old Bridge, Abakkus): .xlsx,
legacy .xls, and zips of workbooks; up to 229 scheme sheets per AMC. One generic heuristic
parser (header detection by ISIN / "% to Net Assets" / "Market value" cues) → 715 schemes.
Layout survey: samples/india_amc/india_amc_layout_survey.xlsx.

Layout drift observed (raw-to-workbook burden): header row anywhere from row 2 to row 12;
ISIN in column 0, 2, 3 or 7; % of NAV as a *percentage* at 6 AMCs and as a *fraction* at 6
(SBI uses both in the same month); values in ₹ lakh; per-scheme sheets with cryptic codes
(T0MD09, FIONF) resolved via an index sheet; UTI's layout defeats the heuristic entirely.

Natural ground truth: each holding's implied net assets (market value ÷ % of NAV) must equal
the scheme's Grand Total. 99.2% of the 715 parsed schemes tie within 0.5%; per-holding
implied values agree within 0.5% for 80% of schemes (the rest is the 2-decimal rounding of % on
small positions, which an agent must recognise rather than "fix").

Workflow scope: see docs/sources/funds_portfolios_pensions.md §I (wealth-client quarterly
review + rebalance across schemes with look-through, overlap, SEBI category-rule compliance,
Regular→Direct switch with TER from AMFI and capital-gains tax). Monthly + fortnightly cadence
makes it contamination-resistant.

## PoC 8: Austin ACFR PDFs → 17-year General Fund panel (poc/acfr_fund_balance.py)

Fetched: 17 City of Austin ACFR PDFs (FY2001–FY2019, ~270 pages each) from the End-of-Term
2020 archive on S3. The discovery-phase index of that archive lists 6,203 ACFR/CAFR PDFs, 3,394
budget books, 872 single audits, 433 actuarial valuations and 167 official statements (2020 crawl,
PDF + OCR present). The parser locates the governmental-funds *Statement of Revenues, Expenditures
and Changes in Fund Balances* in each year and extracts the General Fund column.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Statement located | 15 / 16 dated years (FY2001 layout not found) |
| Within year: beginning FB + net change (incl. special items) = ending FB | 15 / 15 tie to the $1k |
| Across years: ending FB(t) = beginning FB(t+1) | 11 / 12 tie; the one break is FY2011's restated beginning balance (+$19.3M, "as restated, see Note 16"), a genuine, documented exception |

Traps hit (each one broke a check until handled): PDF text extraction splits digits
($ 1 30,163 = 130,163); running-header debris glued to labels (Ye, No) and draft
timestamps (03/27 5:24p); -- for zero and parentheses for negatives; special items
(2004 hospital-district reserve payment, 2014/2019 land sales) create two "Net change" lines;
(See Note 16) gets read as the amount; restated opening balances.

Workflow scope: this is checkpoint 1 of the municipal credit review (W2 in the concept
library). The same PDFs carry the debt, pension and statistical sections; the Data.gov archive
(Harvard LIL, public S3) holds the matching Austin eCheckbook (642 MB) for checkbook-to-ACFR
reconciliation.

## PoC 3/4: N-PORT and Call Reports via Hugging Face datasets-server

Large Parquet mirrors cannot be downloaded (CDN blocked), but the datasets-server /rows and
/filter endpoints are reachable; poc/hfds.py wraps them. Schemas verified:

* trader298/sec-nport: all 29 SEC N-PORT tables (FUND_REPORTED_INFO, FUND_REPORTED_HOLDING
  145.6M rows, MONTHLY_TOTAL_RETURN, DEBT_SECURITY, DERIVATIVE_COUNTERPARTY, …), 2019Q4–2026Q2.
* kairusama/call-report-one: 671,000 bank-quarters, 2001Q1–2026, 224 income/supplemental
  columns, 1,442 canonical columns.

/filter on these multi-GB configs first returns "dataset index is loading" (it took ~10 minutes
of polling for the 0.06 GB FUND_REPORTED_INFO config; the 6 GB holdings config was still warming
when this phase closed).

Pulled (fund level): SERIES_NAME ILIKE '%Target Retirement 2050%' → 160 quarterly reports
across 8 target-date 2050 series (Vanguard, Vanguard Institutional, State Street, Voya, Harbor,
Madison, Victory, ...), 2019–2026; first 100 saved to
samples/nport_target_date_2050/tr2050_fund_level.xlsx.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| total assets − total liabilities = net assets | 100% of 100 reports |
| Net-asset continuity | real break: Vanguard Institutional TR 2050 stops after 10 reports while Vanguard TR 2050 jumps from ~$22B to ~$54B in 2022 (the Institutional→Investor series merger), an event the roll-forward must explain rather than flag as an error |

Next step once the CDN is allowlisted (or the index is warm): pull FUND_REPORTED_HOLDING for
these accessions (a TDF holds 4–6 underlying index funds), then the underlying funds' own N-PORTs
for look-through, which completes checkpoint 1 of workflow F3a.

8. REFERENCE WORKFLOW VIDEO: OTIS 3-STATEMENT MODEL
====================================================================================================

Video   : 3-Statement Model: 90-Minute Case Study from a Blank Excel Sheet
Channel : Mergers & Inquisitions / Breaking Into Wall Street   Length: 1:24:36
Link    : https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=O2uPNfErmJo   Files: https://mergersandinquisitions.com/3-statement-model/
Persona : banking / corporate-finance analyst sitting a timed modeling test
Verified: every number below was checked against the transcript text.

Source documents
  - Otis Worldwide 10-K (the PDF, and the SEC's Excel export of the statements)
  - Otis investor-day presentation (market-share chart, service-unit counts, guidance, capital-allocation targets)
  - Case brief: 5-year projections, revenue built on more than a growth rate, $3B minimum cash, assess the company's claims
Deliverable
  Integrated income statement, balance sheet and cash flow model (historicals + 2022-2026 projections) with debt and
  buyback logic, plus a verdict on management's free-cash-flow and dividend claims.

Time split (chapter markers; 76.7 working minutes between 05:54 and 82:35)
  Enter historical statements   05:54-35:11   29.3 min  (38% of working time, before any modeling)
  Income statement projections  35:11-50:12   15.0 min
  Balance sheet projections     50:12-57:51    7.7 min
  Cash flow projections         57:51-67:12    9.4 min
  Link the statements           67:12-70:59    3.8 min
  Debt and buybacks             70:59-79:16    8.3 min
  Checks, review, verdict       79:16-82:35    3.3 min

Steps (timestamp | step | operations)
  02:44 | Open the 10-K at the financial statements; use the SEC Excel/CSV export rather than copying from the PDF | GATHER
  07:08 | Entry conventions: oldest to newest, revenue positive / expenses negative, fold small lines together | STRUCTURE
  08:11 | Lay out the blank sheet (fiscal-year dates); link income statement lines to the raw export | STRUCTURE, EXTRACT
  13:40 | Net income vs the 10-K: 1,714 vs 1,420; fix reversed signs on non-service pension and interest until it ties | CHECK
  15:31 | Balance sheet condensed to 5-10 lines per side; totals must match reported | EXTRACT, STRUCTURE
  23:24 | Balance check on historical years | CHECK
  24:14 | Cash flow statement with one working-capital line; net change in cash ties to 113, 342, 1,676 | EXTRACT, CHECK
  35:11 | Investor-day deck: market share 16% (2019), 18% (2021) -> ~17% in 2020; service units 2.1M -> 2.5M at ~3%/yr | GATHER, JUDGE
  37:26 | Product revenue = implied market size x market share; service revenue = units x revenue per unit | BUILD
  45:48 | Service revenue vs guidance ($10B by FY2026); growth 4-7% vs "low-to-mid single digit" | CHECK
  46:53 | Costs, tax and non-controlling interest from historical averages | BUILD, JUDGE
  50:12 | Working capital as % of revenue/COGS/OpEx; sanity-check the change in working capital | BUILD, CHECK
  57:51 | Cash flow items: CapEx and D&A as % of revenue; dividends from the stated payout ratio | BUILD
  67:12 | Link statements (cash, PP&E, debt, equity); balance check passes | BUILD, CHECK
  70:59 | $3B minimum cash; excess cash 15% debt repayment / 85% buybacks ("eyeballed"); issue debt on a shortfall; interest on prior-year debt to avoid circularity | BUILD, JUDGE
  79:16 | Model weaknesses: no unit-level drivers, simple service growth | JUDGE
  80:14 | Verdict: 2022-24 FCF ~ $5.2B vs company's $5B target; dividends ~ $1.4B (about 1/3); FCF conversion > 100% (historically 110-133%); growth a bit high; plan is reasonable | DELIVER

Why this is the right level
  - Starts from real, messy primary documents (a filing and a slide deck), not a prepared dataset.
  - Uses all seven operations, in order, in one ~80-minute sitting.
  - Its checks caught real errors (net-income mismatch exposed two sign flips in the source layout).
  - Ends in a verdict, not just a spreadsheet, so there is something to grade beyond formulas.

Other full-chain (fit A) videos and their operation coverage (F = shown, P = partly/by a tool, - = not shown)
