## Workflow: 3-statement model from 10-K

[A] 3-Statement Model: 90-Minute Case Study from a Blank Excel Sheet
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=O2uPNfErmJo
    channel: Mergers & Inquisitions / Breaking Into Wall Street | length: 1:24:36 | uploaded: 20221026 | transcript read: True
    persona: Investment banking / corporate finance analyst (modeling test)
    source documents:
      - Otis Worldwide FY2021 10-K (PDF and SEC 'Financial Report' Excel/CSV version of the statements)
      - Otis investor-day presentation (market share chart, service-unit counts, medium-term guidance, capital-allocation targets)
      - Case-study brief (5-yr projections, revenue must be more than a % growth rate, $3bn minimum cash, evaluate company claims)
    deliverable: Integrated 3-statement model FY2019A-FY2026E with drivers block, debt/buyback logic and a written assessment of management's FCF/dividend claims
    steps:
       02:44  Locate financial statements in the 10-K; prefer the Excel/CSV export over copy-paste from the PDF
       07:08  Data-entry rules: reorder columns oldest->newest, revenue positive / expenses negative, consolidate small line items (e.g., R&D into OpEx)
       08:11  Set up blank sheet (fiscal-year dates and formats) and link income statement lines to the raw export
       13:40  Tie-out: model net income vs reported (1,714 vs 1,420) -> find and fix sign errors on pension and interest lines
       15:31  Balance sheet entry: consolidate to ~5-10 lines per side (cash+restricted, PP&E+goodwill+intangibles because D&A is not split), check totals match reported
       23:24  Add balance check (assets - L&E = 0) on historicals
       24:14  Cash flow entry: one working-capital line, CapEx separate, everything else in CFI combined, single net change in debt; replace '-' dashes that cause #VALUE!; tie net change in cash to reported (113, 342, 1,676)
       35:11  Revenue build from investor presentation: product revenue = implied market size x market share; service revenue = service units x revenue per unit; sanity-check vs guidance ($10bn service rev by 2026, low-to-mid single-digit growth)
       46:53  Cost/tax assumptions as historical averages (COGS % by segment, OpEx %, tax rate, NCI % of NI)
       50:12  Balance sheet projections: working capital items as % of revenue/COGS/OpEx/total expenses; operating lease liability mirrors lease asset; check change in WC stays sensible
       57:51  Cash flow projections: CapEx, D&A, pensions/other as % of revenue; acquisitions and dividends from company guidance (stated payout ratio)
       67:12  Link statements: cash = prior + net change; PP&E roll; debt roll; equity as catch-all for unmatched CF items; balance check passes
       70:59  Debt and buybacks: excess cash over $3bn minimum split 15% debt paydown / 85% repurchases; issue debt if deficit; issuance fees via MAX(); interest on prior-year debt to avoid circularity, rate path informed by 10-K maturity table
       79:16  Review: model weaknesses (no unit drivers), compare cumulative FCF 2022-24 (~$5.2bn) vs company's $5bn target, FCF conversion >100%, growth slightly high
    checks:
      - Net income ties to reported 10-K figure (found two sign errors)
      - Total assets / total liabilities tie to reported for both BS years
      - Net change in cash ties to reported CF statement each year
      - Balance sheet balance check (historical and projected)
      - Change in working capital sign/size sanity check
      - Projected revenue vs management guidance
      - Model FCF/dividends/FCF conversion vs investor-day targets
    judgment calls:
      - Which line items to consolidate vs keep (materiality, how D&A is disclosed)
      - Revenue driver choice given limited data (market share x market size; units x price)
      - Interpolating missing data points (2020 market share)
      - Allocation of excess cash between debt paydown and buybacks (85/15 eyeballed from capital-allocation chart)
      - Interest-rate path given fixed-rate long-dated debt in the maturity table
      - Use prior-year balances to avoid circular references
    maps to our data: SEC filings (generic)
    why useful: Closest public example of the end-to-end 'from the filing' build under time pressure: source extraction with explicit tie-outs, consolidation choices, guidance-based drivers and a final verdict on management claims. Shows ~30 of 90 minutes spent on data entry/tie-out alone.

[A] Microsoft 3-Statement Financial Model From Scratch
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oQTcd-DZ7fc
    channel: ApexKPI | length: 2:39:07 | uploaded: 20260114 | transcript read: True
    persona: Equity / corporate finance analyst
    source documents:
      - Microsoft FY2025 10-K pulled from SEC filings page (income statement, balance sheet, cash flow, statement of shareholders' equity)
      - 10-K footnotes: other income/expense breakdown, property & equipment note (PP&E depreciation), long-term debt maturity table
      - Consensus revenue estimates (Simply Wall St, 51 analysts) for FY26-FY28
    deliverable: Fully integrated 5-year MSFT 3-statement model with D&A, PP&E, non-current asset, debt and interest schedules, balance check, and a street/best/weak scenario switch (OFFSET/MATCH) feeding a later DCF
    steps:
       04:21  Go to Microsoft SEC filings, filter annual filings, open FY2025 10-K (FYE 30 Jun 2025)
       05:32  Set timeline: 3 historical years (IS/CF; BS has 2) + 5 projected years
       07:44  Type income statement line items as reported (product vs service & other revenue/COGS, R&D, S&M, G&A) and hand-key 3 years of historicals
       13:29  Revenue forecast: take consensus estimates for FY26-28, compute implied growth, assume 12.5% for FY29-30 (judgment on AI capex monetisation)
       22:40  Split revenue product vs service with a step-down product share; check segments sum to total
       31:54  Read 'other income (expense), net' footnote to separate interest/dividend income and interest expense into schedules
       42:32  Enter balance sheet line items from 10-K; add rounded balance check row
       46:28  Forecast working capital via DSO/DIO/DPO-style drivers; leave cash, PP&E, debt, equity open
       68:06  Use statement of shareholders' equity to define roll-forwards for common stock/APIC and retained earnings
       70:57  Build cash flow statement from 10-K structure: NI, SBC (grows with revenue), D&A, working-capital asset/liability changes, investing, financing (dividend payout, buyback % of NI)
       91:53  Read long-term debt maturity footnote ($3.0bn FY26, $9.25bn FY27, ...) to drive repayments
      103:09  PP&E footnote: split total D&A ($34bn) into PP&E depreciation ($22bn) vs other; build D&A, PP&E and non-current asset roll-forward schedules
      117:53  Debt schedule: BOP + new debt - repayments = EOP; commercial paper; zero out current portion since maturities are in the schedule
      130:50  Interest income at assumed 3.5% on cash; interest expense schedule
      136:00  Link cash and equity; balance check returns zero -> model balanced
      137:48  Sensitivity tab: street/best/weak cases for revenue growth, segment split, tax rate; scenario switch via OFFSET/MATCH; verify net income moves through all three statements
    checks:
      - Segment revenues sum to total revenue
      - Balance check row (ROUND(assets - liabilities - equity)) zero on historicals and forecast
      - Cash flow items reconcile every balance-sheet movement ('if we do this wrong our model won't balance')
      - Scenario switch propagates through IS/BS/CF
    judgment calls:
      - Use consensus vs own view for revenue; fade growth to 12.5% for out-years
      - Treat other long-term assets as investing rather than working capital
      - Allocate D&A between PP&E and other non-current assets using footnote disclosure
      - Assume no new debt beyond scheduled maturities; payout/buyback ratios from history
      - Interest yield on cash (3.5%) given Fed path
    maps to our data: SEC filings (generic)
    why useful: 2.5-hour real-time build that starts from the actual 10-K on EDGAR and repeatedly goes back into footnotes (other income, PP&E, debt maturities, equity statement) to build schedules - shows the true horizon and number of document look-ups in a from-filing model.

[B] Build a 3-Statement Financial Model [Free Course]
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Rmi9fwkJjHw
    channel: Wall Street Prep | length: 1:34:25 | uploaded: 20220613 | transcript read: True
    persona: Investment banking / corporate finance analyst (training)
    source documents:
      - Hypothetical company historicals typed in as dummy numbers (no filing used; full paid course uses Apple 10-K)
    deliverable: Integrated 3-statement model (3 historical + 5 forecast years) with PP&E/intangibles/equity roll-forwards, revolver and interest, balance check
    steps:
       01:07  Lay out 3 actual + 5 forecast years; hard-coded historicals in blue, center-across-selection, sign convention (expenses negative)
       12:28  Historical ratios block (growth, gross margin, OpEx % sales, tax rate = tax / pre-tax income) -> forecast income statement; leave interest lines shaded for later
       21:06  Enter balance sheet; forecast AR with revenue growth, inventory with COGS growth, prepaid with revenue
       29:36  PP&E and intangibles roll-forwards (BOP + capex - depreciation) using memo lines for D&A buried in COGS/OpEx (from footnotes)
       39:28  Liabilities: AP tied to COGS, accrued to revenue, long-term debt flat, revolver placeholder
       47:05  Equity roll-forwards: common stock/APIC issuance, treasury stock buybacks, OCI flat, retained earnings = BOP + NI - dividends; add balance check
       60:20  Indirect cash flow statement: tick off every balance-sheet line (NI, D&A add-back, working capital, capex, financing)
       78:02  Cash = prior + change; revolver draw/paydown with MIN logic so cash never goes negative; stress test with zero growth / low margin
       89:03  Interest income/expense on beginning balances to avoid circularity; confirm balance check = 0
    checks:
      - Historical PP&E roll-forward won't tie exactly to reported (asset sales, adjustments) - use reported historicals
      - Balance check = 0 after all links
      - Every balance-sheet line ticked off on the cash flow statement
      - Stress case: revolver keeps cash >= 0
    judgment calls:
      - Natural driver for each line (revenue vs COGS)
      - Flat forecasts for non-operating liabilities and OCI
      - Avoid circularity by using BOP balances
    maps to our data: SEC filings (generic) - mechanics only
    why useful: Canonical integration architecture and checks (roll-forwards, tick-off, revolver plug, balance check) but explicitly skips the filing-extraction step ('you didn't have to look at a real company to go through the nuances of data gaps'). Useful as the 'mechanics' half of workflow 1.

## Workflow: Equity research initiation (filings -> model -> valuation -> recommendation)

[A] Build a Real World Financial Model of a Listed Company  in Excel | Complete Tutorial | CA Sanjeev
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2pL3Ktvjf9I
    channel: CA Sanjeev Mittal | length: 3:29:30 | uploaded: 20211025 | transcript read: True
    persona: Equity analyst / financial modeller (Chartered Accountant)
    source documents:
      - Caterpillar Inc. 10-K filings 2016-2020 downloaded from SEC (Excel 'financial report' versions; note mislinked index tabs)
      - 10 years of dividend history; S&P 500 index and CAT price history for beta; debt notes for cost of debt; tax note
      - Current share price
    deliverable: Excel valuation model: WACC (Gordon + CAPM cost of equity, after-tax cost of debt), 5 years of standardised historical IS/BS/CF, rewritten (operating vs financing) balance sheet, ratio-driven pro forma IS/BS 2021-25, FCFF, terminal value, intrinsic value per share vs market price -> BUY recommendation (report delivered as Word document)
    steps:
       02:50  Course map: cost of equity (Gordon, CAPM), cost of debt, WACC -> historical statements -> ratios -> pro forma -> FCFF -> terminal value -> intrinsic value -> buy/sell; report in Word
       10:50  Collect 10 years of dividends, compute dividend growth, cost of equity via Gordon growth model
       24:03  CAPM: expected market return from S&P 500 history, beta by regression on downloaded price data (two methods)
       46:44  Cost of debt from debt disclosures; analyse effective tax rate; after-tax Kd; WACC (6.98%)
       69:20  Download 5 years of 10-Ks from Caterpillar IR/SEC filings in Excel format; find the true income statement table despite broken index links
       74:29  Copy each year's statements into a fixed template (IS, CF, BS) line by line, leaving blanks where the template line does not exist; confirm totals (e.g., total assets 78,324)
      102:45  Rewrite balance sheet into cash, operating current assets, PP&E, long-term receivables, operating current liabilities, debt, equity for ratio analysis; tie to original totals
      111:57  Driver analysis: sales CAGR 2016-19 (11.76%) excluding COVID-hit 2020; operating CA and CL % of sales; net operating cost % of sales (83.5%); gross fixed assets % of sales, depreciation rate; pension and other liabilities growth; dividend growth; tax rate
      132:40  Consolidate all ratios in a single pro forma assumptions block
      138:53  Project IS and BS 2021-25 iteratively (depreciation depends on projected fixed assets); retained earnings roll-forward; treasury stock used as the balancing plug
      185:03  FCFF = PAT + depreciation - increase in operating CA + increase in operating CL - capex + after-tax interest
      200:22  Terminal value at 5.5% long-term growth; discount at WACC to EV; + cash - debt and pension obligations = equity value; / shares = intrinsic value vs market price 193.13 -> undervalued -> BUY
    checks:
      - Template totals tie to reported 10-K totals
      - Rewritten balance sheet ties to original (78,324)
      - Balance sheet balances in projections (but via a treasury-stock plug - a shortcut a reviewer should flag)
      - Ratio consistency across years before averaging
    judgment calls:
      - Exclude 2020 (COVID) from growth base
      - Average vs trend for each ratio
      - Long-term growth rate 5.5% (MD&A, macro)
      - Zero yield on cash; which liabilities are debt-like in the equity bridge
      - Buy/sell threshold vs market price
    maps to our data: SEC filings (generic)
    why useful: Single 3.5-hour, one-company case that covers the whole equity-research chain from downloading 5 years of 10-Ks to a buy recommendation, including the unglamorous data-wrangling (broken EDGAR Excel indexes, template mapping) and a visible modeling shortcut (plug) that a benchmark grader could test for.

    judgment calls:

      - Which KPIs answer a given regulator/investor question
      - Interpreting cost differences (structure vs efficiency)
    maps to our data: #4 FERC Form 1 (PUDL) - exactly the dataset PUDL cleans

## Workflow: FP&A budget vs actual variance & reforecast

[B] Rolling Forecast & Monthly FP&A Cycle — Performance Analysis, Reforecasting & Model Control
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6yplTX1cm1A
    channel: Mohmoud Farag | length: 42:17 | uploaded: 20260730 | transcript read: True
    persona: FP&A analyst
    source documents:
      - Original 2025 budget (forecast = budget at Dec-2024)
      - Monthly actuals for volume, price and revenue loaded each month (simulated data)
    deliverable: Single-tab monthly FP&A model: variance schedules (monthly/quarterly/annual, 2025-26), written variance commentary per schedule, re-baselined 2025/2026 forecast, rolled-forward 2027 budget, and an audited model
    steps:
       00:36  Define the cycle: each month load actuals, explain variance, decide whether 2025/2026 forecast still holds, lock the month and save a new dated version
       02:22  Build volume schedule with dynamic labels and variance / % change at monthly, quarterly and annual levels; data validation and tests
       07:54  Structure graphing data for actual vs forecast trend
       12:21  Revenue schedule aggregates revenue; average price back-solved as revenue / volume
       14:32  January close: new version dated 31-Jan-2025, load actuals for volume, price, revenue; write commentary (volume above plan from marketing; price below on discounting; forecast unchanged pending trend confirmation)
       19:42  March: 3 months running 5-6% above plan -> raise 2025 volume forecast 5% and 2026 by 4%; price below plan 3 months -> cut price forecast 1%; revenue forecast up ~4%
       25:50  December: re-baseline, load final actuals, close the year, note when forecast was last changed
       32:15  Lock prior-period labels, roll schedules into new fiscal year, extend to 2027; set new budget at 9% growth vs 11% actual (judged too aggressive for leadership)
       37:36  Model audit as if inherited: locate all inputs, review formulas column by column, check row consistency across schedules
    checks:
      - Variance and % change at month/quarter/year levels
      - Revenue = volume x price consistency (price back-solved)
      - Version control: dated file per close, prior periods locked
      - Structural audit: inputs located, formulas reviewed by column, no row inconsistencies
    judgment calls:
      - Wait for trend confirmation before changing the forecast (one month of beat may be timing)
      - Revise volume and price in opposite directions with reasons
      - Set budget below last year's growth (9% vs 11%) for credibility
    maps to our data: none
    why useful: Rare depiction of the FP&A loop as a repeated monthly cycle over a year (actuals -> variance -> commentary -> reforecast -> lock -> roll forward), including versioning and a final model audit - directly the long-horizon structure a benchmark needs.

[B] FPA Insights: Variance Analysis
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tLbB22iCtpo
    channel: FP&A and Finance Insights with Jack Alexander | length: n/a | uploaded: n/a | transcript read: True
    persona: CFO / FP&A leader (49-year practitioner, author of 'Financial Planning & Analysis and Performance Management')
    source documents:
      - Driver-based plan with documented assumptions (units, ASP, unit cost, new-product launch timing/ramp, headcount, marketing programmes)
      - Monthly/quarterly actual P&L, balance sheet (inventory, AR), prior-year actuals
      - Weekly AP distribution listings (to spot unplanned large payments)
    deliverable: Variance package: actual vs plan vs prior year with consistent favourable/unfavourable convention, price/volume/mix gross-margin bridge, SG&A and revenue sub-schedules with explanations, balance-sheet (inventory roll-forward) and ROIC/EVA variances, waterfall charts and a top-level narrative with forward implications
    steps:
       02:12  Define comparisons (actual vs plan, forecast, prior year, sequential) and levels (statements, returns, segments, cost centres)
       05:04  Prerequisites: driver-based projections with documented assumptions; accountability with operating managers; FP&A business partners; continuous review (e.g., weekly AP scan for unplanned consulting/legal payments)
       10:14  Start with P&L % of sales and $/% YoY change columns; tie projections tables (product-level ASP, cost, volume, ramp) to actuals
       14:39  Revenue waterfall bridge (new products, geographic expansion, lost business, ASP)
       16:19  Gross-margin 'value-added' variance: volume, price, mix, cost, quality savings - estimate where precision is unavailable
       18:13  Balance-sheet variance: inventory roll-forward (BOP + purchases + labour + overhead - COGS relief) actual vs forecast; root-cause each driver
       20:18  Returns variance: ROIC = NOPAT margin x net investment turnover; explain via margin pressure and AR build from a customer liquidity problem
       22:36  Illustration: summary P&L with consistent fav/(unfav) sign, sub-schedules for SG&A (commissions, delayed launch, unplanned consulting, legal settlement) and product revenue; Pareto-sort to focus on top variances
       28:13  Top-level story: the delayed Product 3 theme across revenue, margin, R&D; forward implications for next year and lessons for the forecasting process
    checks:
      - Consistent favourable/unfavourable sign convention across lines
      - Variances reconcile from sub-schedules to summary
      - Compare to both plan and prior year
      - Pareto focus on variances driving ~80% of total
    judgment calls:
      - Root cause vs math; which variances are timing vs permanent
      - Estimating mix/price effects without precise data
      - Materiality thresholds
      - Forward implications and recommended actions
      - How to improve the forecasting process from recurring variances
    maps to our data: none (SEC MD&A cited as a public analogue of the same analysis)
    why useful: Senior practitioner's anatomy of a professional variance package (calc -> investigate -> explain -> forward look -> process improvement) with concrete schedule formats. Complements the hands-on monthly-cycle video (6yplTX1cm1A).

## Workflow: Quality of earnings / financial due diligence

[B] Adjusted EBITDA Explained for Private Companies
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2WABmktWz44
    channel: FinanceKid | length: 50:43 | uploaded: 20210928 | transcript read: True
    persona: Lower mid-market M&A advisor / sell-side banker (10-12 deals a year)
    source documents:
      - 3 years of private-company income statements (net income, taxes, D&A, interest split long-term vs operating line, gains on asset sales, tax credits, FX)
      - Owner/family payroll and duties; market salary comps (Glassdoor)
      - Lease terms, square footage, market rent per sq ft
      - Facts on one-off events (COVID subsidies, legal/estate fees, division shutdowns, automation capex timing)
    deliverable: 3-year normalised (adjusted) EBITDA bridge from net income, with each add-back/deduction justified - the number the business is valued on (EV = multiple x adjusted EBITDA)
    steps:
       02:16  Reported EBITDA from net income: add taxes, D&A and interest on long-term/shareholder debt only (not operating-line interest, bank or card fees)
       06:02  Screen common normalisation areas: owner salaries, owner benefits, rent, one-time costs/income, subsidies, asset-sale gains, synergies
       10:38  Owner salary: add back actual owner/family pay, deduct market replacement cost for the roles performed (e.g., CEO $125k + bookkeeper $60k)
       16:16  Owner benefits: personal vehicles (pro-rate business use), family travel (agree a business-needed level, add back excess), retirement contributions
       19:44  Rent: normalise related-party or soon-to-reset leases to market ($/sq ft x area difference)
       24:18  One-time items: FX losses only if non-recurring vs history; remove COVID subsidies/one-off grants; one-off professional fees; asset disposal gains/losses (unless equipment-rental business); multi-year R&D credits kept if sustainable
       32:26  Cost synergies: automation headcount savings and facility consolidation added back only for pre-change periods, pro-rated by date; unrealised synergies negotiated (e.g., split 50/50)
       45:02  Final case: 3-year bridge from net income: + taxes, + LT interest, -/+ asset gains, + D&A, rent to market ($5.20 -> $9/sq ft), owner salary to market ($162k -> $175k), + one-off FX loss = adjusted EBITDA
    checks:
      - Each adjustment tested for sustainability vs historical pattern (e.g., FX every year = not an add-back)
      - Timing/pro-rating of synergy add-backs to the period before the change
      - Reasonableness: not too aggressive (lenders will re-underwrite)
      - Bridge shown transparently line by line
    judgment calls:
      - Market salary for owner's actual role/hours
      - Business vs personal share of expenses
      - Whether a subsidy/credit is recurring
      - How much of unrealised synergies to credit
      - Deal leverage affects which add-backs are accepted
    maps to our data: none (private-company financials)
    why useful: Detailed rulebook for the core QoE judgment step (what is and isn't an add-back, with worked numbers and a 3-year bridge). Pairs with O'Connell's process video to cover both the procedures and the adjustment logic.

[B] How to Review a Balance Sheet in Financial Due Diligence
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ab6u0ne8IUk
    channel: Patrick O'Connell - M&A Transaction Services | length: 32:59 | uploaded: 20260121 | transcript read: True
    persona: Transaction services / QoE provider (lower middle market)
    source documents:
      - Target's QuickBooks file: monthly balance sheets Jan-2023 to latest close (accrual basis), by entity for a 3-shop auto-repair group plus opco
      - AR and AP aging detail, bank activity
      - Inventory method (FIFO/LIFO) and intercompany / shareholder loan accounts
    deliverable: Balance-sheet review findings and management question list feeding the NWC analysis (AR collectibility, AP/cash-crunch risk, intercompany schedule, shareholder loans to settle pre-close, entity-level growth assessment)
    steps:
       00:25  Frame the review: cash practices (bank accounts, how cash moves), AR aging, AP and payroll liabilities, inventory method in asset-heavy targets
       07:18  Place BS review in the first 30 days of a 90-day LOI exclusivity window
       09:13  Run 3 years of monthly balance sheets from QuickBooks (accrual basis)
       11:03  Spot AR swing (685k -> 553k -> 328k -> ~1m) and ask about payment-term changes, new customers, collectibility; expect matching revenue spike
       14:47  Cash declining ~$1m while AR rises -> unpack bank activity
       15:27  AP fell from $1.4m to ~$65k -> vendors demanding faster payment -> cash-crunch risk on day one
       19:27  Multi-entity review: compare AR, inventory, AP, accrued payroll, accrued expenses by shop; focus time on the most material entity
       25:13  Intercompany balances should net to ~0 in consolidation - build an intercompany schedule; exclude current intercompany items from NWC
       27:06  Shareholder loans: identify and require settlement before close
    checks:
      - Monthly trend review for material period-over-period changes
      - AR movement consistent with revenue movement
      - Intercompany nets to zero on consolidation
      - Line of credit terms and debt-like items identified
    judgment calls:
      - Materiality - where to spend limited diligence time (largest entity)
      - Whether AR spike is collectible or a terms change
      - Which intercompany items belong in NWC
    maps to our data: none (private-company QuickBooks ledgers)
    why useful: Shows analysts actually reading real monthly multi-entity balance sheets and turning anomalies into questions and NWC adjustments - the investigative middle step of a QoE that the other two videos summarise.

[B] Financial Due Diligence Essentials
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJ8rZ6L1pd4
    channel: Patrick O'Connell - M&A Transaction Services | length: n/a | uploaded: n/a | transcript read: True
    persona: Transaction services / QoE provider (lower middle market)
    source documents:
      - Read-only access to target's QuickBooks (monthly P&L, balance sheet, GL detail)
      - 3 years of monthly bank statements for all cash accounts
      - AR/AP aging reports
      - Diligence request list (~10 items)
    deliverable: QoE report: diligence-adjusted EBITDA bridge with write-ups per adjustment, net working capital analysis and peg recommendation, proof of cash/revenue, findings used for purchase-price negotiation (retrade)
    steps:
       02:16  7-step FDD process: planning and data request (week 1 of ~4), analytical procedures, IS review, BS review, then QoE, NWC and proof of cash
       10:12  NWC analysis from reported balance sheet: current assets - current liabilities, then remove cash (cash-free) and debt-like items (debt-free) to get diligence-adjusted NWC
       13:07  Average NWC by year (e.g., $860k in 2023 vs $1.4m in 2024 growth year) and choose peg based on forward revenue expectations
       16:02  P&L walkthrough with management: line-by-line YoY variance on QuickBooks P&L, drill into GL accounts behind big moves, live note-taking
       18:19  Balance sheet review: AR/AP month-end swings, pull agings, identify aged items to write off
       20:33  Proof of cash/revenue: bank receipts less non-operating items (transfers, tax refunds) vs QuickBooks revenue adjusted for change in AR; target within +/-1-2%; proof of expense for under-reporting
       24:34  QoE: reported net income -> EBITDA -> diligence adjustments (one-time professional/transaction fees, owner auto expense, out-of-period items, revenue adjustments) with written support -> adjusted EBITDA
       29:04  Use findings to negotiate purchase price / working capital (retrade)
       30:36  Checklist essentials: QuickBooks access + bank statements enable all core tests
    checks:
      - Proof of cash: bank receipts vs accrual revenue +/- AR change within 1-2% ('fraud test')
      - Proof of expense vs disbursements
      - NWC averages vs revenue trajectory
      - Aged AR/AP review for write-offs
      - Each EBITDA adjustment documented and traceable
    judgment calls:
      - Which current liabilities are debt-like
      - Which averaging window sets the NWC peg
      - Whether an expense is one-time / owner-related (add-back)
      - Cash-to-accrual conversion for cash-basis sellers
      - How hard to push a retrade
    maps to our data: none (private-company ledgers + bank statements; structurally similar to #2 government ledgers)
    why useful: Practitioner walk-through with screenshots of real-deal NWC, proof-of-cash and adjusted-EBITDA schedules; defines the inputs (GL + bank statements), the ordered analyses and the explicit tolerance (1-2%) - directly convertible into benchmark tasks and checks.

## Workflow: Spreading financial statements from annual reports

[A] How to Get Real Financials Into Your Model (Spreading Explained)
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lzg8xQkdF3s
    channel: Nico Schwarz | length: 24:40 | uploaded: 20260824 | transcript read: True
    persona: Investment banking associate (M&A); same task as credit/equity spreading
    source documents:
      - Nestle (and Danone) consolidated annual report / financial statements PDFs (IFRS), incl. notes 3.1 (D&A) and 15 (EPS/WASO)
      - Company IR-published broker consensus, MarketScreener consensus screenshots
      - Investor presentation / capital markets day guidance
      - Share price and market data
    deliverable: Standardised input tabs for acquirer and target (IS, BS, CF, net debt, EV bridge, multiples, consensus forecasts) with every hard-code source-commented and checks rolled up to a control tab; feeds operating model, DCF, LBO and merger model in later parts
    steps:
       01:26  Set general assumptions on input tab (currency, market-data date) linked to a single control tab
       02:12  Hand-key consolidated (group, not segment) income statement 2023-25, expenses negative, oldest->newest; add a cell comment with the source page for every blue hard-code
       04:52  Choose 3-5 historical years (go further back if COVID-distorted) and compute growth and % of sales
       05:58  Define one standard line-item structure usable for both companies; bucket distribution, marketing, admin, R&D into 'operating expenses' plug and document what is included
       06:41  Pull D&A from note 3.1 because EBIT is reported without it; build EBITDA from 'underlying trading profit' to exclude non-recurring items (normalisation)
       08:06  EPS from weighted average shares (note 15); DPS uses period-end shares
       09:19  Spread balance sheet; bucket small items into other current assets/liabilities; sums are formulas not hard-codes; check vs reported totals
       11:13  Compute total debt incl. IFRS 16 lease liabilities, net debt, net debt/EBITDA, implied cost of debt on average balances
       13:16  Spread indirect cash flow statement; bucket small items; reconcile net change in cash incl. cash held for sale (742 vs 740) and check it matches BS cash movement
       15:44  Market data and EV bridge (market cap + net debt + NCI - equity-accounted investments); implied EV/EBITDA, P/E
       20:17  Fill consensus estimates (company-published broker consensus, then MarketScreener); keep internal arithmetic consistent when broker counts differ by line
       23:03  Sense-check consensus against company guidance (3-4% growth vs 3.4% consensus); repeat identical structure for target
    checks:
      - Sum-based subtotals compared with reported totals (no hard-coded totals)
      - Balance sheet check rolled up to control tab
      - Cash flow net change in cash ties to balance-sheet cash (explaining held-for-sale reclass)
      - Consensus vs management guidance sense check
      - Source comment on every hard-coded number for auditability
    judgment calls:
      - Bucketing line items into a common structure for comparability across companies
      - Using 'underlying' profit to strip one-offs (normalisation)
      - Treatment of IFRS 16 leases as debt
      - Which consensus source to trust; handling inconsistent broker counts
    maps to our data: Annual-report PDFs (IFRS) - analogous to SEC filings (generic) / UK Companies House accounts
    why useful: Explicit, practitioner-level description of spreading as its own stage: standard template, source-tagging, normalisation, multi-year checks and consensus overlay. Part 2 of a ~10-part series (control tab -> spreading -> operating model -> DCF -> comps -> precedents -> LBO -> credit rating -> merger verdict) that shows the full horizon.

## Workflow: Spreading financial statements from annual reports (credit normalisation)

[B] Credit Rating Analysis for M&A: The S&P Method
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tX2Ujv4Ic6k
    channel: Nico Schwarz | length: 24:01 | uploaded: 20261009 | transcript read: True
    persona: M&A / leveraged finance associate doing a rating-agency-style credit analysis
    source documents:
      - Spread financials and operating model (from annual reports) for Nestle standalone and pro forma combined with Danone
      - Annual-report notes: pension deficit, share-based compensation, dividends from associates, cash and short-term investments, interest, taxes, deferred tax
      - S&P published corporate methodology, CICRA/business-risk tables and financial-risk benchmark matrix
    deliverable: S&P-adjusted debt, adjusted EBITDA and FFO; FFO/debt and debt/EBITDA, interest cover; anchor rating with financial-policy and comparable-rating modifiers -> standalone (AA-) vs pro forma (A+ falling to A- in grace period) rating path
    steps:
       01:35  Pull inputs from control tab, spread historicals, consensus, operating and merger model tabs
       03:10  Business risk profile via CICRA (country/industry risk) and competitive position -> 'excellent' for Nestle; choose medial volatility table
       04:46  Assumptions for financial adjustments: unfunded pension deficit as debt (net of tax), share-based comp deducted from EBITDA, dividends from associates added back
       07:13  Surplus cash: deduct 1.5% of revenue operating cash, then 10% trapped cash (emerging-market capital controls) before netting
       09:31  Financial-policy modifier (-1 notch for debt-funded deal, lapses after 18-24 month grace period); comparable-rating modifier (+1 for scale/brand)
       11:06  Derive ratios from history (SBC % sales, associate payout 60.8%); use 2024 not distorted 2025; document judgement in cell comments
       12:41  Build adjusted net debt (gross financial debt + pension deficit net of tax - surplus cash after trapped cash) standalone and pro forma
       15:53  Adjusted EBITDA and FFO = adjusted EBITDA - cash interest - cash taxes (incl. deferred-tax unwind from merger purchase-price allocation)
       20:41  Map FFO/debt and debt/EBITDA into S&P matrix -> financial risk profile -> anchor -> modifiers -> rating; compare standalone vs pro forma over years
    checks:
      - Adjusted metrics reconcile to spread statements and model tabs by column/year
      - MAX() guards against negative surplus cash
      - Ratios vs S&P benchmark thresholds
      - Judgement documented in comments for reviewers/refresh
    judgment calls:
      - Operating cash requirement and trapped-cash %
      - Which year is representative (2024 vs distorted 2025)
      - Notching modifiers
      - Treatment of pensions and SBC
    maps to our data: Annual-report PDFs (IFRS) - analogous to SEC filings (generic)
    why useful: Shows what credit analysts do after spreading: agency-style normalisation of debt/EBITDA/FFO and rating mapping, with explicit judgement calls - extends workflow 2 to the credit deliverable.

## Workflow: Utility rate case / revenue requirement

[C] Utility Rate Making 101
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V2qLIgg8Yxc
    channel: E4 Carolinas | length: 1:31:44 | uploaded: 20200722 | transcript read: True
    persona: Regulatory staff (NC Public Staff attorney) explaining how the consumer advocate audits a rate case
    source documents:
      - Utility rate case application: exhibits, testimony, proposed rates
      - Historical test-year financial data (12 months) and pro forma updates
      - Responses to discovery/data requests (200+ multi-part requests in a Duke case)
      - Depreciation study (asset lives), cost of capital evidence (proxy group, credit ratings), class load data (coincident peaks)
    deliverable: Staff testimony and exhibits with proposed adjustments -> revenue requirement, cost allocation to classes, rate design; Commission order
    steps:
       16:09  Rate case timeline (270 days): utility files application, testimony and rates; Commission suspends; public staff discovery, audit of books and records; intervenor testimony; settlement; hearings; proposed orders; order
       21:01  Test-year principle: actual cost of service for a recent 12 months, adjusted for non-recurring/non-representative costs; pro forma update through a cut-off (e.g., Dominion: TY 2018 updated to 30-Jun-2019) leaving time for staff audit
       25:03  Revenue requirement = rate base x rate of return (grossed up for income tax) + expenses
       26:11  Rate base = original cost of used-and-useful plant (incl. capital additions) - accumulated depreciation + working capital; excess capacity disallowed
       29:06  Rate of return: cost of debt (coupon, credit ratings), cost of equity (DCF, CAPM, risk premium on proxy group), capital structure (may be imputed, e.g., 50/50)
       35:22  Worked example: 55% equity at 10%, 45% debt at 4% -> WACC 7.30%; gross up equity for 40% tax -> pre-tax 10.97%; x $3bn rate base = $329.1m pre-tax return
       37:06  Expenses: O&M, depreciation (lives from 2018 depreciation study: nuclear 50y, transmission 60y, AMI meters 15y...), taxes, fuel, purchased power; prudence test
       38:54  Allocate revenue requirement to classes (residential/commercial/industrial) and jurisdictions (NC vs SC ~65-70%) using 1CP / summer-winter peak & average methods
       43:12  Rate design by class: fixed vs variable charges, TOU, real-time, demand charges; bill breakdown
       50:52  Other items: CWIP, AFUDC, abandoned/early-retired plant, regulatory assets/deferrals; annual riders (fuel, REPS, DSM/EE)
    checks:
      - Used-and-useful and prudence tests on plant
      - Normalisation of test-year costs (remove one-time items)
      - Capital structure reasonableness (imputation)
      - Revenue requirement arithmetic (weighted cost, tax gross-up 1/(1-t))
    judgment calls:
      - Allowed ROE from competing models
      - Imputed capital structure
      - Which test-year costs are non-representative
      - Cost allocation method (1CP vs average & peak)
      - Fixed/variable split in rate design
    maps to our data: #4 FERC Form 1 (PUDL) - rate base (plant in service, accumulated depreciation), O&M, depreciation and capital structure are all FERC Form 1 schedules
    why useful: Gives the regulator-side anatomy of a rate case and the revenue-requirement formula with a worked numeric example - the spec a FERC-Form-1-based benchmark task (rebuild a utility's revenue requirement / earned ROE) would follow.

## Workflow: Utility rate setting / revenue requirement

[B] Rate Setting Using the Water and Sewer Rates Analysis Model
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YZ7B5BDuB74
    channel: Environmental Finance Center at UNC-Chapel Hill | length: n/a | uploaded: n/a | transcript read: True
    persona: Small water/sewer utility finance director or rate consultant
    source documents:
      - Current rate sheets (base charge, consumption allowance, up to 5 volumetric blocks) for residential, commercial, irrigation
      - Customer billing history by account (monthly consumption) to derive volumes sold per block
      - Account counts and growth, tap/impact fees, bad-debt rate
      - Existing-year operating expenses (inflated) and known fixed items (debt service, cash-funded capital) by year
      - Beginning fund balance and target minimum balance
    deliverable: 20-year projected end-of-year fund balance and revenue/expense ratio under existing vs proposed rate structures; recommended rate adjustment for board
    steps:
       10:40  Model logic: beginning fund balance + projected rate revenue - expenses = ending fund balance, rolled 20 years
       22:10  Enter existing fiscal year and rate structures (base charge, allowance, block thresholds and $/kgal, unit conversions from cubic feet)
       27:51  Enter proposed rate structure side by side (e.g., uniform -> increasing block, +$4 base)
       28:24  Enter accounts, growth, connection fees, uncollectible %
       30:27  Expenses: known year-specific items (debt service, capital) vs base-year opex inflated at an assumed rate; beginning fund balance
       33:21  Derive monthly volume sold per block from account-level billing data (split each bill into block quantities, sum, /12)
       35:31  Set demand assumptions: long-term per-capita decline and price elasticity (~3% use reduction per 10% rate increase)
       36:29  Interpret charts: fund balance vs target, revenue/expense ratio > 1; iterate proposed rates until targets met; review annual cash-flow sheets
    checks:
      - Fund balance never below zero / target
      - Revenue-to-expense coverage ratio > 1
      - Block thresholds increasing; volumes per block consistent with billing data
    judgment calls:
      - Choice of base year
      - Inflation and demand-decline assumptions
      - Price elasticity
      - Target fund balance level
      - Rate structure design (uniform vs block)
    maps to our data: #2 government ledgers (municipal utility funds); not FERC Form 1
    why useful: Only located video that operates an actual rate-setting workbook end to end (billing data -> revenue by block -> cash-needs projection -> rate iteration). It is the municipal cash-needs approach, not the IOU rate base x return revenue requirement, so it covers half of workflow 5.

## Workflow: Government fund accounting: GASB 34 conversion, fund balance, year-end close, budget-to-actual

[B] Comprehensive Example: Preparing Government Wide Financial Statements | CPA exam FAR
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qRHDkeXv8Sk
    channel: Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses | length: 50:40 | uploaded: 20170802 | transcript read: True
    persona: CPA instructor demonstrating the GASB 34 conversion worksheet used by government preparers/auditors
    source documents:
      - Fund-basis statements for fictional Jefferson County FY2017 (governmental funds balance sheet and SRECFB; enterprise and internal service fund statements)
      - Facts: prior capital assets $7.89M net, depreciation $316K, capital outlay $651,900, $700K 6% bonds + $22,800 premium, $22K principal, $1,800 premium amortization, unavailable property tax $22,600 (prior $19,500), transfers $65,800, ISF profit $5,660
    deliverable: Government-wide statement of activities and statement of net position (governmental activities) in Excel
    steps:
       00:32  Worksheet: fund totals -> adjusting entries -> government-wide column
       02:41  Re-establish prior-year capital assets (Dr capital assets / Cr beginning net position)
       05:29  Book depreciation $316K
       07:42  Remove capital outlay expenditure $651,900 and capitalize
       10:15  Replace bond proceeds/premium OFS with bonds payable $700K + premium $22,800
       15:00  Reclassify principal payment $22K to reduce bonds payable (678K)
       17:46  Accrue interest 678K x 6% x 3/12 = $10,170
       20:32  Amortize premium $1,800 against interest expense
       22:47  Recognize unavailable property tax $22,600; reverse prior-year $19,500 via beginning net position
       26:15  Eliminate interfund transfers $65,800
       28:26  Fold in internal service fund; eliminate internal profit $5,660 against expenses; add ISF interest $600
       39:14  Compile statement of activities (change in net position $31,705)
       42:33  Compile statement of net position; NIICA = capital assets net - related debt & premium; restricted from fund statements; unrestricted residual
       48:10  Check assets = liabilities + net position; fix a mislinked inventory cell
    checks:
      - Each entry balances
      - Capital outlay and OFS lines zero out after adjustment
      - A = L + NP on final statement
      - Totals flow from worksheet links
    judgment calls:
      - Which debt is capital-related for NIICA
      - ISF profit elimination allocation
      - Restricted vs unrestricted classification
    maps to our data: #1 ACFR PDFs (reconciliation schedules give answer key) and #2 ledgers/trial balances
    why useful: Only screen-share of the fund->government-wide conversion workbook; every adjustment type a city preparer makes, with numbers; directly templatable as a benchmark task (fund statements in, government-wide out).

[B] Annual Comprehensive Financial Report (ACFR) Preparation Guide
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wW3hW_sqRP8
    channel: Cherry Bekaert | length: 58:38 | uploaded: 20231025 | transcript read: True
    persona: Government accounting advisory MD (ACFR writing) and audit partner / former GASB practice fellow (Cherry Bekaert)
    source documents:
      - Prior-year issued ACFR with account mapping
      - Current-year preliminary and final trial balance
      - GFOA general-purpose checklist, GAAFR Appendix D, state templates

    deliverable: Issued ACFR (introductory, financial, statistical sections) submitted for GFOA certificate
    steps:
       04:29  ACFR anatomy and audiences (citizens, investors/creditors, oversight bodies)
       08:58  GASB Concept Statement 7: keep only essential note disclosures; prune legacy schedules
       29:07  Common MD&A errors: amounts not tied after late adjustments; no reasons for changes
       32:32  Statement errors: NIICA components, nonspendable inventories/prepaids, only GF positive unassigned, no negative restricted/committed/assigned, annual major-fund test
       34:46  Notes/RSI: encumbrances by fund, aggregate pension/OPEB schedule tying to face, covered payroll vs covered-employee payroll, contribution timing vs measurement date

      - Pension/OPEB aggregate ties to face
      - Check figures between statements zero out
      - Prior-year issued ACFR ties to mapping (no lingering plugs)
    judgment calls:
      - Which disclosures are 'essential'
      - Fund balance classification rationale
      - Level of MD&A analysis
    maps to our data: #1 ACFR PDFs; #2 ledgers (trial balance import)
    why useful: Practitioner checklist of the ACFR build and its most common errors; ready-made list of automated checks for a benchmark grader; gives human time (5-8+ weeks).

[A] Let's Insert the Numbers USA 🦅 (Part 5) | Finance Decoder Tutorial
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yAbiyx0cgM8
    channel: Strong Towns Library | length: 16:17 | uploaded: 20250822 | transcript read: True
    persona: Local fiscal-health analyst (Strong Towns Finance Decoder volunteer/staff) transcribing a US county ACFR into a standard ratio template
    source documents:
      - FY2024 ACFR PDF of a US county (GFOA-award report; name garbled in captions as 'Kier County') - MD&A condensed statement of net position and changes in net position (current + prior year columns)
      - Note 6 Capital Assets (governmental and business-type: not-depreciated, depreciated/amortized gross)
      - Strong Towns Finance Decoder Google-Sheet template (US version)
    deliverable: Completed Finance Decoder workbook (2 fiscal years entered, raw ratios and 7 trend graphs) shared view-only with the requesting member and submitted to strongtowns.org/decoder
    steps:
       00:00  Open ACFR PDF; jump to MD&A (p.24) where condensed net position is; note GFOA certificate means better navigation
       01:04  Set template units to 'millions' to match the report; enter current and prior year side by side from the comparative MD&A table
       01:35  Current and other assets (governmental + business-type combined) 2,360.3 / 2,156.9; PDF text is OCR'd but not copyable, so key by hand
       02:41  Capital assets net 3,159.9 / 3,037.8; sanity-check combined total assets (5,195) against report
       04:22  Deferred outflows 147.5 / 131.7 and deferred inflows 66.1 / 41.7
       05:33  Total liabilities (not just current; long-term is the focus) 1,395.1 / 1,421.9
       06:11  Changes in net position: total revenues 1,488.8 / 1,493.9; operating grants 89.7 / 116.2; capital grants 108.7 / 99.9
       08:29  Interest on long-term debt 10.4 / 10.3
       10:17  Go to Note 6: governmental capital assets not being depreciated 852.1 / 790.7 and being depreciated (gross) 2,658.7 / 2,544.0
       13:05  Business-type capital assets: not depreciated 248.4; depreciated 1,896.1 / 1,811.6 (US sheet separates GA/BTA, Canadian sheet combines)
       14:49  Review computed raw ratios/graphs; share sheet view-only and submit link
    checks:
      - Unit consistency (thousands vs millions) set once in template
      - Read every hand-keyed number back aloud / double check (typo risk because PDF text not copyable)
      - Recompute total assets and compare with report total (rounding tolerance)
      - Same line item wording as template label; governmental vs business-type columns
      - MD&A capital assets net should equal Note 6 total net of depreciation
    judgment calls:
      - Which ACFR line maps to each template field (e.g., 'current and other assets' as financial assets)
      - Whether to combine governmental + business-type activities
      - When the report does not subtotal, compute it yourself
    maps to our data: #1 municipal ACFR PDFs (GovText archive)
    why useful: Closest real screen-recording of ACFR -> spreadsheet extraction: exact line items, prior-year comparatives entered together, manual keying pain, unit choice. Roughly 15 min per fiscal year of inputs; the template expects a 10-year series, so a full task is ~2-3 h of extraction plus interpretation (Part 6).

[B] Let's Interpret The Graphs (Part 6) | Finance Decoder Tutorial
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=me1fyWbfu9M
    channel: Strong Towns Library | length: 32:18 | uploaded: 20250823 | transcript read: True
    persona: Municipal finance writer (Michel Durand-Wood, 'You'll Pay for This') interpreting the decoder ratios with a council-oriented user
    source documents:
      - Completed Finance Decoder sheet for City of Delta, BC (2012-2024 entered from annual financial reports; Canadian PSAB statements)
    deliverable: Ratio-by-ratio interpretation narrative (trend, threshold, plausible causes) used to brief council/community
    steps:
       00:02  Fill years working backward from the most recent report so restated comparatives (2022 restatement) are used
       01:10  Net financial position: want positive and rising; no universal target, compare to own history
       03:21  Financial assets / total liabilities (2.2x, down from 2.4-2.6x a decade ago): improving but slowing
       08:16  Explain depreciation as the forcing function for setting money aside; pair with net-book-value ratio
       12:38  Total assets / total liabilities: below 1 = insolvent (Houston, NYC, Detroit cited); Delta ~8 but falling = getting poorer
       17:29  Net debt / total revenue: net debt = negative net financial position (not borrowing); years of revenue to close the gap
       21:16  Interest / total revenue (flexibility): 2-3% manageable, rising or 4-5% is a warning; spreadsheet shows 0 due to whole-number format
       23:57  Net book value / cost of tangible capital assets (~60-65%): falling = consuming infrastructure; growth, developer-contributed assets and inflation push it up
       27:43  Government transfers / total revenue (vulnerability): Delta 2-6% vs 20-25% elsewhere; Detroit example
       31:29  Wrap-up: read the 10-year trend line, not single points
    checks:
      - Use restated prior-year figures
      - Gaps in years create false dips
      - Number formatting can hide sub-1% ratios
      - Ratio must be >1 for solvency
    judgment calls:
      - Trend vs level; own-history comparison over peers ('cleanest shirt in the laundry')
      - Context adjustments for growth/inflation in asset-consumption ratio
      - Whether high intergovernmental dependence is strategy or risk
    maps to our data: #1 municipal ACFR PDFs (same ratios computable from any ACFR government-wide statements)
    why useful: Shows the interpretation layer and concrete thresholds analysts actually apply to ACFR-derived ratios; pairs with Part 5 to form a full doc->sheet->conclusion chain.

    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Fh4JZCB-Aus

    deliverable: Major program determination workpaper (programs 1 and 2 selected) feeding the compliance test plan
    steps:
       01:02  Build complete program list from SEFA (roll prior SEFA forward); total federal expenditures $3.55M
       02:09  Step 1: Type A threshold $750K (total < $25M); classify each program; clusters count as one program
       03:52  Step 2: risk-assess Type A; one not audited as major in last 2 years -> high risk; two low-risk Type A
       04:59  Step 3: assess high-risk Type B = at least 1/4 of low-risk Type A count (2 x 25% -> 1); skip Type B below 25% of Type A threshold ($187,500); stop once found
       09:37  Step 4: majors total $1.4M vs 20% coverage for low-risk auditee ($710K); add programs if short
       12:24  Test compliance and internal control for each major program
    checks:
      - Program list complete vs SEFA
      - Clusters aggregated
      - Coverage percentage met (20% low-risk / 40% otherwise)
    judgment calls:
      - Type A risk factors (prior findings, recent major-program audit)
      - Type B high-risk factors (new program, multiple control systems)
      - Avoid over-assessing extra Type B
    maps to our data: SEFA data (Federal Audit Clearinghouse) - not among listed sources; ACFR single-audit sections in #1 sometimes include SEFA
    why useful: Excel worked example of the rule-based step that drives single-audit scope; fully gradable (deterministic thresholds). Note: thresholds shown are pre-2024 Uniform Guidance; 2024 revision raised the single-audit and Type A floors, so tasks must fix the regime by fiscal year.

[B] Materiality: Opinion Units in Audit Planning | Harshwal & Company LLP Training
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5GD4WGr5NXQ

## Workflow: Fund accounting: NAV calculation and custodian reconciliation (fund administrator)

[B] Fund Accounting: NAV & HWM Calculation Process/Fund accounting end-to-end NAV calculation example
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JZPV88cTbZk
    channel: GMT Academy-Learning First | length: 1:07:46 | uploaded: 20230315 | transcript read: True
    persona: Fund-accounting trainer (GMT Academy) working a live NAV and high-water-mark example with trainees, then outlining the NAV package
    source documents:
      - Trial-balance-style list of fund assets and liabilities for an existing multi-class fund (equity at book and market value, T-bills, investment in a master fund 57,800, prepaid fees)
      - Net loss for the period; units outstanding; base high-water-mark NAV
    deliverable: Total assets, total liabilities, NAV per unit, and incentive-fee/crystallization conclusion (NAV below HWM -> no incentive fee; losses allocated to unit holders)
    steps:
       00:01  Read the case: identify all asset types, compute total assets, then NAV per share; multi-class (A-D) existing fund
       03:21  High-water mark defined on a base price; no incentive fee unless NAV exceeds it
       04:59  Line 1: equity investment - choose market (fair) value over book value; record the change through P&L (FVTPL)
       09:58  Add T-bill / government-security investments and subtotal the investment lines
       11:38  Add investment in master fund (57,800) and prepaid fees as assets
       13:37  Book the net loss (debit losses) and absorb it in shareholders' equity
       18:47  List liabilities and outstanding unit capital
       23:47  Assets and liabilities do not tie -> question the missing cash-at-bank component before striking NAV
       27:10  Rule: do not compute NAV per share while the balance sheet is out of balance
       32:22  NAV per unit = (total assets - total liabilities) / units outstanding
       34:03  Quarterly HWM test: NAV (gross asset value 2,231.75) far below base -> no incentive fee, no crystallization
       42:44  Allocate losses to existing unit holders
       59:40  NAV package components: capital stock activity (subscriptions/redemptions), trade processing/settlement, corporate actions, income distribution, reconciliations, fund set-up
    checks:
      - Balance sheet must tie before NAV is struck (find missing cash)
      - Fair value used for listed investments
      - HWM comparison before accruing incentive fee
    judgment calls:
      - Book vs market value treatment
      - Where to absorb the period loss
      - Whether incentive fee crystallizes
    maps to our data: none (synthetic trial balance); positions could be seeded from SEC N-PORT
    why useful: Only video found that walks a numeric NAV strike with a tie-out failure and HWM test; long, classroom-style and partly off-topic in the second half.

[B] Cash Reconciliation
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HuMT8inUjJs
    channel: The Fund Accountant | length: 20:43 | uploaded: 20220910 | transcript read: True
    persona: Fund accountant (The Fund Accountant channel) explaining daily cash reconciliation between fund accounting, custodian and investment manager
    source documents:
      - Fund-accounting cash ledger by currency (contractual settlement basis)
      - Custodian cash statement by currency (actual settlement basis)
      - Investment-manager instructions (trades, FX contracts, expenses); corporate-action and dividend data
    deliverable: Daily cash reconciliation by currency with every break researched, commented and followed up (chasers to custodian/IM) so the NAV can be struck on clean books
    steps:
       00:32  Define parties: fund accounting vs custody (internal or external), sometimes three-way with the investment manager
       01:38  Why breaks arise: FA books on contractual dates (trade T+2, dividend pay date) while custody books only on actual cash movement
       04:25  Research and comment every break the same day; un-researched breaks become aged backlog
       06:04  Reconcile each currency balance separately (USD base plus GBP, AUD, INR...)
       06:37  Dividend payment breaks: query custody after the team's waiting period (1-3 days)
       07:42  Failed/late trade settlement breaks: confirm custody is chasing the broker
       09:22  Currency-contract breaks: IM failed to instruct custody; ask IM or send instruction
       10:29  FX-rate breaks: contract booked on different dates/rates; verify your own booking before blaming custody
       14:20  Asset set up in different currency at FA vs custody -> offsetting breaks in two currencies; resolve by dummy FX or settlement-currency change per team practice
       16:30  Corporate-action cash (reclaims, tenders): check receivable bucket before posting to avoid double counting
       18:10  Expense payments instructed only to custody: reduce accrual and book payment when custody processes
       18:56  Daily method: confirm prior-day balances agree, list today's settling items (trades, dividends, capital activity) and match line by line
    checks:
      - Prior-day FA vs custody cash agree before working today
      - Line-by-line match of settling trades, income and capital activity
      - Per-currency balance agreement
      - Receivable bucket checked to avoid double-booking reclaims
      - Break ageing / follow-up cadence
    judgment calls:
      - Who is wrong (FA or custody) on FX/booking breaks
      - How long to wait before chasing
      - Which resolution entry matches team practice
    maps to our data: none (needs synthetic custodian vs ledger files); N-PORT holdings could seed positions
    why useful: Precise catalogue of break types and the daily matching procedure - directly convertible into a reconciliation task with planted breaks.

[B] Position and Income Reconciliation
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=I5NuvmTP-E4
    channel: The Fund Accountant | length: 14:43 | uploaded: 20220917 | transcript read: True
    persona: Fund accountant (The Fund Accountant channel) explaining position and income reconciliation
    source documents:
      - FA holdings by security/asset line; custodian holdings; IM instructions
      - Broker statements for futures/options
      - Dividend accruals (receivables) at FA and custody; ex-date trades; withholding-tax data from third-party vendors
    deliverable: Position and income reconciliations with commented breaks (trade, corporate action, asset line, futures, ex-date, withholding tax, cancelled dividend) prior to NAV sign-off
    steps:
       00:20  Position recon: holdings FA vs custody (or vs IM), same treatment for all parties
       01:02  Trade breaks: missed cancellation, duplicate booking - show in positions before cash (T+2)
       03:04  Corporate-action breaks (rights, spin-offs, mergers) from different dummy-line treatment
       03:52  Asset-line set-up differences between FA and internal custody
       05:28  Futures position breaks: request broker statement; late instructions after NAV cut-off booked next day and commented
       08:04  Income recon at accrual stage (receivables) to pre-empt cash breaks
       09:17  Ex-date trade breaks: entitlement on pre-trade vs post-trade shares; verify full amount at payment (e.g. $85 received vs $100 due)
       10:55  Withholding-tax breaks: confirm taxability from external vendor; escalate to reviewer
       12:09  Cancelled dividends: custody drops accrual; FA must verify with vendor and reverse, else it becomes a cash break
    checks:
      - Holdings agree by security
      - Accruals agree before pay date
      - Payment equals entitlement
      - Withholding tax consistent with vendor data
      - NAV cut-off for late instructions
    judgment calls:
      - Escalation to reviewer/senior on tax treatment
      - Comment vs correct a justified break
    maps to our data: none (synthetic); positions could be seeded from SEC N-PORT holdings
    why useful: Completes the reconciliation rule set (positions + income) needed to design a NAV-pack task.

[C] Portfolio Performance Attribution:  The  Brinson-Fachler Model
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TSWKwcv42QI
    channel: Ric Thomas - Campus Macro | length: 10:08 | uploaded: 20211222 | transcript read: True
    persona: Institutional investment professional / educator explaining performance attribution
    source documents:
      - Portfolio and benchmark sector weights (three sectors) for one month
      - Portfolio and benchmark sector returns
    deliverable: Brinson-Fachler attribution table: allocation, selection and interaction effects by sector that sum to the 1.8% excess return, with a verbal interpretation
    steps:
       00:32  Compute portfolio return (3.6%) and benchmark return (1.8%) as weight x return sums; excess return 1.8%
       01:38  Allocation effect per sector = (wp - wb) x (rb_sector - rb_total): consumers +7 bp, financials +63 bp, technology +31 bp; total 101 bp
       04:21  Selection effect = wb x (rp_sector - rb_sector): +38 bp, -132 bp, +111 bp; total +17 bp
       06:28  Interaction effect = (wp - wb) x (rp_sector - rb_sector): consumers +12 bp, technology +15 bp, financials the remainder (about +33 bp, large underweight where selection was bad); total ~60 bp
       08:38  Summary table by sector and effect; sums to ~178-180 bp = excess return
       09:14  Interpretation: better allocators than stock pickers; poor selection in financials; one month too short to act on
    checks:
      - Sum of allocation + selection + interaction must reconcile to total excess return (rounding)
    judgment calls:
      - Do not draw manager-quality conclusions from one period
    maps to our data: #5 SEC N-PORT (holdings-based sector weights for a fund vs index) - possible input
    why useful: Precise computational spec and tie-out for the attribution step inside a due-diligence memo; concept-level only (no real fund data).

[A] How to Review a Client’s Mutual Fund Portfolio ? | Live MFD Demo (FinApp)
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=p_m2DCJi520
    channel: FinApp by Pulselabs | length: 1:41:11 | uploaded: 20261010 | transcript read: True

    source documents:
      - Detailed CAS (consolidated account statement) PDF from CAMS/KFintech - explicitly not the NSDL/CDSL summary CAS
      - Client risk-profile questionnaire (risk capacity, risk tolerance, risk required)

      - Scheme vs benchmark returns
      - RTA mailback files for distributor's existing clients (bulk alternative to CAS upload)
    deliverable: Client-shareable PDF portfolio review: suitability verdict at asset-allocation, category and scheme level; folio-wise scorecard with highlighted problem schemes; proposed switches with old-vs-new portfolio comparison (holdings, correlation, short-term gains/tax impact, a cost/risk metric 3.44 -> 2.80)
    steps:

       25:06  Run client risk profiler: separate risk capacity, risk tolerance and risk required to reach goals

       32:00  Review totals invested and current value; portfolio risk profile vs client risk profile
       33:41  Folio-wise analysis of all 28 schemes; 'blood test report' framing - out-of-range items highlighted for discussion
       37:04  Scheme ranking is a combination of 21 factors (returns plus risk etc.); keep only schemes in first quartile (top 25%) of category
       38:50  Scheme vs benchmark: e.g. 10.5% vs 12.42% and rank 6 of 6 -> underperformed on both tests -> exit candidate
       40:27  Impact rating (minimal / high positive / high negative) driven by the scheme's weight in the portfolio
       42:41  Debt/liquid funds are not ranked the same way (strategic holdings)
       43:13  Time-window choice: recent investors (<3 years) need shorter ranking windows
       47:04  Portfolio optimize: full rebalancing (hard) or targeted switches; pick replacement by category or favourite schemes (e.g. move a pure-equity thematic holding to hybrid)
       48:45  Submit -> old vs new portfolio analysis: holdings, correlation, transactions and short-term capital gains
       49:55  Generate PDF and share with the client
    checks:
      - Use the detailed CAS (transaction-level) rather than the summary CAS
      - Benchmark comparison and category rank must both fail before recommending exit
      - Short-term capital gains / tax shown before switching
      - Ranking window must fit the client's holding period
    judgment calls:
      - Suitability vs client's stated and required risk
      - Exit vs hold for an underperformer, weighted by its portfolio impact
      - Replacement scheme choice (advisor's preferred list)
      - How to present findings to the client (doctor/blood-report analogy)
    maps to our data: #6 India AMC monthly portfolios + AMFI NAV/TER (scheme returns, category ranks, holdings for overlap/correlation); client CAS would need to be synthesized
    why useful: The most complete source-doc -> analysis -> client deliverable chain found for India; shows the practitioner's real inputs (detailed CAS), decision rules (quartile + benchmark), and the deliverable (PDF review with switch plan). [B] Client Portfolio Review as Mutual Fund Distributor
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=12U_M7zEL7I
    channel: MentorShobhit | length: 16:08 | uploaded: 20241215 | transcript read: True
    persona: Trainer of mutual fund distributors (MentorShobhit) teaching the client portfolio-review routine

      - Quartile rankings by category from AdvisorKhoj (calendar-year quartiles)
      - Client goal/life-event changes
    deliverable: Review conclusions: asset re-allocation trades (book profit in overweight debt, add to equity) and keep/replace decision per scheme based on quartile consistency
    steps:
       00:00  Purpose of review: performance check, keep/exit schemes, strengthen relationship, cross-sell/up-sell
       04:30  Maintain risk-reward: asset allocation drives ~90% of returns
       07:52  Start from risk profiling; set target allocation (example 60/20/10/10)
       09:32  Compare current (55/25/10/10) to target; rebalance by booking profit in debt and moving it to equity ('asset re-allocation')
       11:15  Scheme review: each scheme should be in its category's top quartile; judge only after at least one year
       13:38  Use AdvisorKhoj quartile ranking page; sort by category
       14:14  Keep a fund if top/upper-middle quartile in at least 2-3 of the last 3-4 years (e.g. JM Flexicap, HDFC Flexicap); otherwise consider replacement
    checks:
      - Target allocation sums to 100%
      - Minimum one-year evaluation period
      - Quartile consistency across years
    judgment calls:
      - Rebalance trades vs tax/exit load
      - How many years of quartile slippage justify replacement
      - Goal or family changes that alter the target
    maps to our data: #6 India AMFI NAV (category quartile ranks computable from NAV history)
    why useful: Gives the MFD's standard operating rules (allocation drift, quartile consistency). Hindi auto-captions only.

## Workflow: Portfolio rebalancing (drift vs target, bands, trade list, tax-aware, model portfolios)

[A] Portfolio Rebalancing Calculator Tutorial - Measure Twice Planners
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=G6KoHtjdGio
    channel: Measure Twice Planners | length: 15:06 | uploaded: 20241018 | transcript read: True
    persona: Advice-only financial planner (Measure Twice Planners network member) whose DIY clients place their own trades; he runs the calculator live with them
    source documents:
      - Current balance of each holding per account, keyed from the custodian (client screen-shares custodian login)
      - Bank checking/savings cash outside investment accounts
      - Desired allocation per ticker/asset category for each account (taxable, pre-tax, Roth)
      - Desired cash remaining after trades (including planned withdrawals, e.g. $20k from taxable, $50k from pre-tax)
      - Knowledge of which taxable positions carry unrealized capital losses (sold first)
    deliverable: Per-account dollar trade list in two variants (full rebalance 'trades with sale' and buy-only 'trades without sale'), plus household-level asset-allocation (detailed and US/foreign/bonds/cash) and tax-location (taxable/pre-tax/tax-free) summary, current vs post-trade
    steps:
       00:02  Purpose: run the calculator live while the client logs into the custodian so both see which trades to place; also handed to clients for future self-rebalancing
       00:34  Enter checking/savings cash so household-level reports include cash held outside brokerage/retirement accounts
       01:40  Taxable brokerage block: list tickers and asset categories (US large/mid/small, foreign developed, emerging, bonds, cash), incl. funds to be bought; enter desired allocation (80/20 example) until the red 'not 100%' warning clears
       02:43  Enter current balance per holding; compare computed account total with the actual account; current allocation = balance / account total
       03:15  Set desired post-trade cash (e.g. fully invest the $20k cash position)
       03:47  'Trades with sale' column = desired $ - current $ (sell US large/small, buy mid/foreign/EM/bonds); future allocation equals target
       04:19  'Trades without sale': allocate only available cash to underweight holdings pro rata (percentage-of-cash column) to avoid realizing gains; result moves closer to target (bonds 12% -> 19% vs 20% target) but not exact
       05:21  Tie-out: current and future account totals must be equal
       05:53  Withdrawal case ($20k cash need, no cash in account): with-sale variant oversells to raise cash and rebalance; buy-only variant flags in red that cash cannot be raised without selling
       06:26  Practice note: first sell taxable positions with unrealized capital losses, then re-enter balances including freed-up cash before computing trades
       09:09  Pre-tax and Roth blocks: same layout without the tax-avoidance columns (e.g. 60/40 with $50k withdrawal; Roth moved to 100% equity by selling bonds and deploying cash)
       10:48  Add more accounts by copying a block and re-pointing absolute references (warns about broken fixed references)
       13:03  Household roll-up: sum each asset category across accounts, current vs future (with and without sale); red highlight if roll-up totals disagree
       14:09  Tax-location summary: dollars and % in taxable / pre-tax / tax-free (no change from trades because money does not move between accounts)
    checks:
      - Target allocation per account must sum to 100% (red warning otherwise)
      - Computed account total should match the custodian's account value
      - Current total = future total per account (trades are self-financing apart from requested cash)
      - Red flag when requested cash cannot be raised in the buy-only variant
      - Household roll-up totals must agree across current/future views (red highlight on mismatch)
    judgment calls:
      - Full rebalance (realize gains) vs buy-only cash deployment in taxable accounts
      - Harvest unrealized losses before rebalancing
      - How much cash to hold or withdraw and from which account (tax location)
      - Fund/ticker chosen per asset category (presented as examples, not advice)
    maps to our data: none directly (client custodial positions); a benchmark task could synthesize multi-account household holdings
    why useful: Clearest end-to-end advisor rebalancing workbook found: multi-account household, explicit target/current/trade/future columns, tax-aware buy-only variant, withdrawals, and built-in tie-out checks. Good template for a 'rebalance trade list' deliverable.

[B] AdvisorClaw Series: Advisor-built tax rebalancer for Schwab & iRebal
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mxJuJ8KEdMc
    channel: IDX | length: 15:29 | uploaded: 20260908 | transcript read: True
    persona: RIA portfolio manager (quant, ~10 years running rebalancers) doing tax-sensitive rebalancing of inherited low-basis accounts

[B] How to Make a Portfolio Rebalancing Spreadsheet
    https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9qgSqXiK1nM
    channel: The White Coat Investor | length: 10:36 | uploaded: 20191104 | transcript read: True
    persona: DIY physician investor / personal-finance educator (White Coat Investor)
